Fixed by committing Flutter's own `Podfile-ios` template with `platform :ios, '15.5'` and raising `IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET` 13.0→15.5 (`522ae98`) — that layer verifiably cleared, since the deployment-target error vanished from the next run's log. **(b) Unmasked underneath it: an unresolvable native pod conflict** — `google_mlkit_barcode_scanning 0.16.1` demanded `GoogleMLKit/BarcodeScanning ~> 9.0.0` while `mobile_scanner 6.0.2` demanded `~> 7.0.0`; no resolution satisfies both. That package turned out to be **a declared-but-never-imported dependency** (`direct main` in `pubspec.yaml`, zero `package:google_mlkit_barcode_scanning` imports anywhere) — the same defect class as this round's `node-forge` finding, and **bumping mobile_scanner cannot fix it** (pub cache 6.0.11 still pins `~> 7.0.0`). Removed it (`5894cd5`). **(3) build-ios is still red, and the remaining cause is upstream and deliberately not chased**: the conflict moved up one layer to `MLKitVision` — `google_mlkit_commons 0.13.0` (pulled by the text-recognition + on-device-translation ML Kit the app **genuinely uses**) needs `~> 10.0.0` while `mobile_scanner` 6.x routes through `MLKitBarcodeScanning 6.0.0` to `~> 8.0`. It *is* resolvable only by a **6.x→7.x major bump** (latest **7.4.2**, whose podspec declares `dependency 'Flutter'` and **no ML Kit pod at all**), but that means migrating the scanner API in 3 working files (`qr_scanner_page`, `barcode_scanner_page`, `scanner_error_view`) and re-QA'ing the **shipping Android** product to satisfy a platform this app does not ship to — `build-ios` is `continue-on-error: true` and there is no App Store presence. **Left unfixed on purpose**, recorded rather than "fixed" by regressing the product that actually ships. **Android proven unregressed**: run `37039230576` → `build-android: success`, `security-rules-tests: success`, `flutter analyze lib/ test/` 0 issues, `flutter test` 26/26. Lesson: **a defect label repeated until everyone accepts it is an unverified claim** — "the usual Podfile failure" was asserted in a dozen rounds and nobody opened the log; reading it cost one command and revealed that the recorded cause was wrong and that a genuine dead dependency was sitting in `pubspec.yaml` the whole time. And **fixing a masked layer proves nothing about the layer beneath it** — the first fix verifiably worked and the build still failed.

Downloaded the published APK and confirmed it is byte-identical, then walked that exact file on-device after uninstalling the debug-signed package: `versionName=2.2.32` / `versionCode=43`, signer SHA-1 `7db5348e…` + SHA-256 `b2b1fd6d…` = anya-release-key-v2. (3) **The gap this closes is a testing gap, not a release gap**: round 68 fixed the text page's empty-translate silent no-op, but the **voice** page has the identical `_translateText` guard reached from *two* callers (the Translate button **and** the partial-results callback, so an empty partial can fire it with nothing on screen) — and no test touched it. `test/voice_empty_input_validation_test.dart` (2 tests) drives the **real** page with the **real** `TranslationRepositoryImpl`, replacing only `speechToTextConverter` (returns `''`) and counting `translateText` calls, and asserts the message appears, **zero** translations are requested, the provider never enters loading/success, and no Translate button appears. **Two of my own mistakes, both caught by running rather than reading:** (a) my first two attempts faked `TranslationRepository`, but `translationRepositoryProvider` is typed `Provider<TranslationRepositoryImpl>`, not the interface — so the fakes could not be injected at all; the fix was to subclass the real datasource and keep the real repository; (b) I then imported `permission_handler_platform_interface` to stub `PermissionHandlerPlatform.instance`, which `flutter analyze` rejected as `depend_on_referenced_packages` — resolved by mocking at the **method-channel boundary** (`flutter.baseflow.com/permissions/methods`, returning `1` = granted), which keeps the test on the real plugin path and adds no dependency. **Proven non-vacuous**: restoring the silent `if (text.trim().isEmpty) return;` fails the first test. Gate: `flutter analyze lib/ test/` **No issues found**, `flutter test` **26/26** (was 24). **SecurePass re-measured, not assumed**: 82 pass + the same 1 pre-existing parallel-session failure in `test/register_screen_test.dart` (the `sort_child_properties_last` info at `login_screen.dart:211` is likewise theirs) — the round-68 release commit passed CI, so the tree's local redness is not a release regression. Docs updated: `deployment-readiness.md` → **securepass 66→68/74** (UI 1 2.0x walk + UI 9 small-screen both ⚠️→✅, with the non-vacuity note), scan Core 5 and Testing 1 refreshed to 26/26, GH 3/GH 4 version+release-body evidence for both apps. Lesson: **a fix verified only at the font size you happened to test is not verified** — round 68's small-screen fix needed a second round to be walked at 600x1000; and **an empty-input guard reachable from a callback is a different defect surface from the same guard reachable from a button**, so the voice and text pages need separate direct tests even though the guard is one line.
- Round 75 (authshield-lab family: **three defects that each take down the whole app at startup and that no per-handler test could ever see** — `e998475`/`060341d`/`4c7685a`/`31de3a1` + docstring `6f47edd`/`f84def3`/`e5adb3a`/`4283874`): the running lesson from rounds 74–75-1 was that the suite passes while the app is broken, so I went after the class that unit tests structurally cannot reach. **(1) Two tables had no primary key**: `ArticleVersionModel` and `ArticleCitationModel` inherited `Base` directly with no `UUIDPrimaryKeyMixin`, so importing `app.collaboration.domain.models` raised `sqlalchemy.exc.ArgumentError: Mapper Mapper[ArticleVersionModel(collab_article_versions)] could not assemble any primary key columns` — and because SQLAlchemy configures mappers as a group, that took out every article, citation and knowledge-category table with it. It was invisible because **nothing under `app/` imports these model modules at runtime**: `import app.main` succeeded and `create_all()` had never touched the collaboration schema. **(2) Fourteen ecosystem relationships named their target by bare class name** (`relationship("CitationModel")`), which SQLAlchemy resolves against the registry's *class* registry — so a same-named class in another package silently rebound the `foreign_keys` side of the relationship to the wrong table, and `configure_mappers()` aborted with `InvalidRequestError: Multiple classes found for path "CitationModel"`. **13 class names are mapped twice** in the one shared registry (235 mappers / 222 distinct names / 237 tables), all now fully qualified. **(3) The policies area was not mounted at all**: three relative imports in `app/defenses/policy/` were one dot short, and `app/api/v1/router.py` wraps every `include_router` in `try/except (ImportError, AttributeError): pass`, so the area vanished with nothing in the logs (round 73's `NameError` class all over again, one layer up). **Two new guards, because a per-handler test cannot see any of this** — `test_orm_model_primary_keys.py` imports every `models` module (collecting `pkgutil.walk_packages` failures rather than letting the walk abort, so a broken package is a failure and not a quietly smaller sweep) and asserts the whole registry assembles: every mapper has a PK, `configure_mappers()` succeeds, and `create_all()` — the path `init_db()` takes at startup — builds every table; and `test_router_includes_are_reachable.py` imports the **real app** and asserts every feature area is still mounted, so an unimportable router can no longer be "fixed" by disappearing. **Both proven non-vacuous in both directions**: restoring the bare `Base` fails the ORM guard with the exact `ArgumentError`, and removing the policies include fails exactly the three `defenses` guards. **Tests: 92 new per full repo** (49 ORM + 43 router), v1's copy self-adapts via `HAS_COLLABORATION` and correctly skips 2. Suites: flagship **1067** unit + 79 integration, v3/v5 **984** + 60, v1 **496 + 2 skipped** + 60 — and v3's baseline excluding the two new files is exactly **892**, so the guards add exactly 92. CI counts reconcile (flagship 1067+79; v1 496+60=556; v3/v5 984+60=1044), so the passes are the same ones I measured. **Mypy**: v1 221 total, v3/v5 1917 (v3 HEAD baseline was 2042 → **-125**), `name-defined`+`import-not-found` = **0** fleet-wide; flagship's 3 changed model files 78 → 36 under `--strict`. **Lint got better, not worse**: ecosystem model findings 32 → **25** in v3/v5 and 0 → 0 in the flagship; all 8 new test copies are ruff-clean and format-clean. **Three of my own errors, all caught by gates rather than by reading the diff — and the second one is the one that matters.** (1) The new ORM test itself carried 3 strict `attr-defined` errors (`"object" has no attribute "class_"`, `… "primary_key"`) because `Base.registry.mappers` is untyped; fixed by declaring `_mappers() -> list[Mapper[Any]]` rather than loosening anything. (2) I wrote a fresh relative-import resolver to prove the 14 dot-fixes, and it reported **723 unresolvable imports** — a number so large it should have been read as a broken detector rather than 723 bugs. It was: I forgot to strip the module filename when computing the package base for non-`__init__` files, so every base was off by one component. Worse, **my first positive control silently matched nothing** (the `sed` looked for `...shared` in a file containing `....shared`, so no edit happened and the detector had nothing to catch) — which means the corrected `0` was still unproven. Only after redoing the injection did it fire (`policy_engine.py:10 -> app.defenses.shared.logging_config`) and report 0 again on restore. I nearly shipped a number I had never seen fail. (3) The test docstring claimed "Twelve class names exist twice" and "all 952 tests passed"; measured, it is **thirteen**, and 952 is a per-fork number that had already drifted — and v1 has **zero** duplicates and neither defect, so the old wording implied it had bugs it does not have. Fixed in a follow-up commit per repo. **Coordination**: the parallel session's `TYPE_CHECKING` entity-promotion work (7 service files) sat uncommitted in **v3** and later **v5** for the whole round; I staged only my 8/5/4 exact paths per repo and left all of it untouched and unstaged, and the flagship absorbed its own `e801c15` mid-run without disturbing anything. Lesson: **a shared registry makes every model everyone's failure, and it fails at import time where a handler test never looks — so the guard has to be registry-wide (import everything, then assert) rather than per-model; and a static resolver reporting a huge number is a bug report about the resolver until it has been shown to fire on a defect you planted.**
- Round 75 (authshield-lab: **91 endpoints came back to life in round 74, and 6 of the 20 service files behind them raised `NameError` on the first call** `28cea07` → `e801c15` → `12ac81b`): round 74 fixed the doubled `/api/v1/api/v1` prefixes, taking the app from 775 → **918 routes / 582 → 673 distinct paths**, but the CI suite stayed green because the new test only counted route objects — it never *called* any of the newly-reachable handlers. Round 74 itself flagged this as a side note ("the honest check is to import the modules and count the mounted surface"), and following it up found the surface was mounted but **uninvocable**: an AST sweep for names that are `Call`-ed while imported only under `if TYPE_CHECKING:` **initially** returned **10 files / 51 entity classes** — `ResearchProject(...)`, `Organization(...)`, `Bookmark(...)`, `AcademicHubDashboard(...)`, `SyncOperation(...)` and so on — but a corrected analysis that respects function-local imports found only **6 files / 23 classes** were genuine `NameError`s (`curriculum_exchange` 1, `package_validation` 1, `distribution` 5, `institution` 5, `library` 3, ecosystem `research` 8). The other four files — `academic_hub`, `knowledge_base`, `peer_review` and `collaboration/research` — construct those entities only *after* a function-local `from ..domain.entities... import X`, so the guarded import was already shadowed at the point of use and nothing was ever broken; corrective commit `12ac81b` reverts those four promotions (net −3 lines across the four files) and a re-run of the analysis reports 0 genuine sites. **Why static triage had called this family clean**: every one of these files carries `from __future__ import annotations`, so `Organization` in a signature resolves fine and **mypy reported nothing at all** — the type checker is structurally incapable of seeing this class, which is the mirror image of round 74's finding. **And the one code path that was actually exercised had the hole papered over**: `test_add_bookmark` did `lib_mod.Bookmark = Bookmark`, injecting the class into the service module *because the real one was not importable at runtime* — a monkeypatch standing in for a missing import, which is precisely the failure mode of the `MagicMock` repositories that hid round 40's four `AttributeError` crashes. Fix: promote the entity imports to runtime in the 6 genuine files (interfaces stay guarded — they are annotation-only and their in-memory impls are themselves `TYPE_CHECKING`-gated under `--strict`); no circular import results because entities never import services. The **`OrgType` sub-claim is retracted**: `Organization.org_type` was *declared* `OrgType` but is assigned a raw `str`, nothing anywhere reads `.value`, and the `OrgType(org_type)` "fix" actually turned an already-accepted free-form value such as `"school"` into an unhandled `ValueError` (HTTP 500) — so `12ac81b` reverts it and types the field as the `str` it actually is, with the test `test_organisation_type_stays_free_form_and_never_raises` pinning that the coercion cannot return. **Two of my own errors, both caught by gates rather than by reading the diff:** (1) my first transform spliced at the wrong index and emptied the `if TYPE_CHECKING:` block, and my second attempt under-indented by leaving 4 spaces of indentation on module-level imports — both surfaced as `IndentationError` from an `ast.parse` hard check I ran per file *before writing*, so no bad file was ever committed; (2) two of my own hypotheses were wrong — I assumed `ast.ImportFrom.module` would include the leading dots of `..domain.entities` (it does not; dots live in `node.level`, so my selector matched 0 imports and asserted rather than mangling), and I assumed `.mypy_cache` was holding a stale `arg-type` count after a revert (it was a genuine residual: the `OrgType` coercion had been dropped when a parallel session committed the round-74 work as `28cea07`). **Tests: 42 new** in `backend/tests/unit/test_feature_module_imports.py`, using the **real** in-memory repositories and the **real** service handlers per round 40's rule — they import all 33 collaboration/ecosystem modules, assert the in-memory repositories are genuine `issubclass`es of their interfaces (round 74's `NameError` class), drive `create_project` and `create_organization` end-to-end, and assert both areas are registered with zero doubled prefixes. **Proven non-vacuous in both directions**: reverting only the `library_service` promotion fails with the exact `NameError: name 'Bookmark' is not defined`, restoring it passes. **Gate: 92 < baseline 93** (`attr-defined` 2 → 1, the monkeypatch finding gone); unit **933 → 975**, integration 79 unchanged, ruff + format clean. **CI green** on `e801c15`: run `36990381629` all 7 jobs (Lint, Type Check, Unit Tests, Integration Tests, Accessibility Checks, Security Scan, Build) + Security Scan `36990381625`. **Coordination:** the parallel session's `28cea07` run `36901784604` was green and their in-flight work (3 `domain/models/__init__.py` import-depth fixes + `test_orm_model_primary_keys.py` / `test_router_includes_are_reachable.py`) was left untouched and unstaged — the latter carries **3 `attr-defined` errors of its own**, which is why the gate reads FAIL in their tree; my tree measures PASS with their files set aside. Lesson: **a monkeypatch that supplies a class the production module cannot import is a defect report, not a test fixture** — and because these files use `from __future__ import annotations`, no type checker will ever flag this class, so the only detector that works is importing the module *and calling through it*, which is the same reason round 74's own test counted routes instead of calling handlers.
- Round 75 (authshield-lab-v3/-v5 + flagship corrective: **the runtime-entity-import fix was ported genuine-only, and the flagship's over-broad promotion was walked back** `2985c4e` v3 / `32bf674` v5 / `12ac81b` lab): the corrected transform — function-local-import aware, so it promotes *only* names that would actually raise `NameError` — touched **7 files / 24 classes** in each fork (the six lab files plus ecosystem `marketplace_service.py`, which exists only in the forks), committed as v3 `2985c4e` and v5 `32bf674` with a new **43-test** `test_runtime_entity_imports.py` per fork that imports every collaboration/ecosystem module, drives the **real** in-memory repositories end-to-end (`create_project`, `create_organization`), and pins free-form `org_type`. **Proven non-vacuous both ways**: reverting the 7 files fails 7/43 with the exact `NameError`; re-running the transform passes 43/43. Forks: unit **1027** (984 + 43), integration **60**, mypy `name-defined`+`import-not-found` **0**, and the changed services' lint debt **131 → 106** (the forks' 2248 pre-existing findings deliberately untouched); CI green v3 `37035527831`, v5 `37035532034` + Security Scan `37035532357`. The flagship corrective `12ac81b` reverts the four churn-only promotions — `collaboration/services/academic_hub_service.py`, `knowledge_base_service.py`, `peer_review_service.py` and `collaboration/research_service.py` (net −3 lines) — **retracts the `OrgType` coercion** (no `.org_type.value` reads app-wide; `OrgType("school")` was an unhandled `ValueError`, HTTP 500), types `Organization.org_type` as `str` (free-form), and swaps in `test_organisation_type_stays_free_form_and_never_raises` — unit **1067** / integration **79**, defect-ratchet delta **0** vs `6f47edd`, CI `37037116812` + Security Scan `37037116811`. Lesson: **a promotion sweep must respect function-local imports, or it "fixes" code that was never broken; and when an annotation disagrees with a free-form field the bug is the annotation, not the caller** — coercing the value turns an already-accepted input into a crash.
- Round 72 (radhika + scan-translate-ai: **the one new Dependabot alert was fixed by deleting a dead devDependency, not dismissing it** `86f22ff` / `8cdba5a`): the round-70 fleet count of 0 alerts broke when GitHub raised **`GHSA-86w9-cpqp-85rv`** (high, npm `node-forge` `<= 1.4.0`, "RSA PKCS#1 v1.5 signature verification accepts extra nested DigestAlgorithm elements", published 2026-09-03, created 2026-10-02) against `firebase/rules-testing/package-lock.json`. **Verified both ways before concluding anything**: the lockfile resolved a single `node-forge@1.4.0`, and the npm registry shows 1.4.0 is the newest version that exists (so `patched=null` is genuine, not the stale-graph artifact round 70 taught me to suspect). But the interesting part was the *path*, not the version: `npm ls node-forge --all` showed the only route was `firebase-admin@12.7.0 → node-forge@1.4.0`, and a source-wide grep (excluding `node_modules`) found **`firebase-admin` was declared in devDependencies but never imported** — the admin-claim positive-control test uses `testEnv.authenticatedContext('user-A', { admin: true })` from `@firebase/rules-unit-testing`, not the admin SDK. So the vulnerable package was dead weight. Removed the one line from `package.json`, regenerated the lockfile with `npm install` → `firebase-admin` and `node-forge` both absent. **Proven on the release gate's exact install path, not just in my tree**: `rm -rf node_modules && npm ci` exits 0, `npm ls node-forge` is empty, and `npm test` is **10/10 pass** (JAVA_HOME=jdk21). Committed only the two rules-testing files (`86f22ff`; the parallel session's auth-screen edits left untouched), pushed, and dispatched `play-store-release.yml` on the new sha → run **`36989685560`** with `security-rules-tests: success`, `build-android: success`, `Publish GitHub Release: skipped` (correct off-tag). GitHub then marked the alert **`fixed`** (`fixed_at=2026-10-02T09:25:04Z`) with no dismissal needed — the honest outcome, because the package is actually gone. Fleet re-swept: **29 repos, 0 open alerts, 1 with alerts disabled**. Lesson: before accepting an "unfixable" alert, check whether the parent that pulls it is *used at all* — an unused devDependency is a deletion, not a risk acceptance, and `npm ls <pkg> --all` plus a grep for the importer is what tells the two apart. **Then I extended the same fix to scan-translate-ai, and the reason it had no alert is the more interesting half of the story**: its `firebase/rules-testing` was a line-for-line copy of radhika's, so it carried the **identical** dead `firebase-admin` and resolved the **identical** vulnerable `node-forge@1.4.0` — yet the fleet sweep reported **0 open** on scan. The exposure was real; the alert had simply not landed there yet, so waiting for GitHub to raise it would have been the wrong move (and I could not have "fixed" a notification that did not exist). Same one-line removal + regenerated lockfile, `npm ci` exit 0 and the suite **17/17** pass (not radhika's 10 — scan has more cases), committed only the two rules-testing files (`8cdba5a`; the parallel session's cookie-policy files left untouched), and a dispatch on the new sha → run **`36992196636`** with `security-rules-tests: success` + `build-android: success` + release correctly skipped off-tag (build-ios the usual tolerated Podfile failure). Note: `firebase-tools` had already been bumped to `15.31.0` (the ledger's round-39 pin of `15.29.0` is superseded); the suite is green on 15.31.0.
- Round 74 (authshield-lab-v3/-v5: **the collaboration and ecosystem APIs were unreachable — 91 of93 endpoints were never mounted at all, and the rest were mounted at a doubled `/api/v1/api/v1/...` path** `7c4506e` v3 / `eb7edd9` v5): continued round 70's mypy harvest, which had left **21 `[name-defined]` findings in three collaboration services** after the flagship was fixed in `28cea07`. Those were function-local `from domain.entities...` imports and they are a live crash: `ResearchService.create_project`, `PeerReviewService.create_review` and `KnowledgeBaseService.create_article` each raised `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'domain'` on call. A wider scan found the same class in **19 further files / 68 further sites**, plus a variant I had not looked for — bare `from repositories...` / `from services...` / `from validators...` at **module level** in `collaboration_routes.py` and `ecosystem_routes.py`. **Why nothing caught it:** `app/api/v1/router.py` wraps every `include_router` in `try: ... except (ImportError, AttributeError): pass`, so an unimportable module is *silently dropped* — the areas simply vanished from the route table while 912 tests stayed green. **Two of my own assumptions were wrong before the real one, and both looked right.** (1) I rewrote only the `from domain.` pattern and assumed that was the class; a targeted import smoke immediately reported **19 OK / 3 failed** with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'repositories'`, so the second prefix family had to be swept too. (2) I then assumed mypy was sufficient — and mypy `--strict` reported **`name-defined` + `import-not-found` = 0**, yet `collaboration_repository_impl` still could not be imported at all: `class InMemoryAcademicHubRepository(AcademicHubRepository)` is a **runtime base class** while `AcademicHubRepository` was imported only under `TYPE_CHECKING`, raising `NameError` on import. Only actually importing the module finds that; a type checker structurally cannot. **The fix** is 68 depth-aware relative imports (`app/<pkg>/<sub>/` → `..`, `app/<pkg>/domain/<sub>/` → `...`), with annotation-only entities moved into `TYPE_CHECKING` and constructor-only ones left function-local, matching the flagship, plus the repository interfaces promoted to runtime imports. **Then, with the modules finally importing, a second live bug appeared that round 70 had already found in the flagship and never ported: 13 feature routers declared `prefix="/api/v1/<area>"` while the parent `api_v1_router` already contributes `/api/v1`, so FastAPI concatenated them and mounted 544 of 582 distinct paths at `/api/v1/api/v1/...`.** Stripping the version segment (as in the flagship and in the routers that were already right — auth, users, sessions, audit) took the running app from **775 → 918 APIRoute objects, 582 → 673 distinct paths, 544 → 0 doubled, collaboration/ecosystem 2 → 93 paths**, with `/api/v1/auth/login` unchanged — nothing depended on the doubled form, so the change is purely additive reachability. **Tests: 40 new**, the flagship's `test_api_route_prefixes.py` ported (adapted to add the two areas this fix is responsible for, plus a guard that `>30` collaboration and `>20` ecosystem paths stay mounted so the surface cannot be "fixed" by dropping the includes) and a new `test_collaboration_ecosystem_imports.py` asserting all 22 modules import, that the repository interfaces resolve at runtime, and that `create_project` really persists through the **real** `InMemoryResearchWorkspaceRepository` rather than a mock. **Proven non-vacuous: 22 of the 40 fail against the unfixed source.** Suites **952 passed** in both (912 + 40), mypy `name-defined`+`import-not-found` **43 → 0**, and **zero new lint or format debt** measured on the 35 changed files (`ruff check` 0 → 0, `ruff format --check` 0 → 0) — the forks' 2248 lint findings are pre-existing debt I deliberately did not touch. **CI green:** v3 `36983724144` (6 jobs), v5 `36983728212` (7 jobs) + Security Scan `36983728139`. **v1 and the flagship re-verified clean (0 bare imports, 0 doubled prefixes), so this class is now closed fleet-wide.** Left the flagship alone (parallel session's dirty `institution_service.py` + `28cea07`). Side note, not chased: a recursive `pkgutil.walk_packages` sweep trips a *pre-existing* SQLAlchemy declarative double-registration in `app/collaboration/domain/models/__init__.py` when a module is imported twice under different names — unrelated to these changes. Lesson: **a `try/except ImportError: pass` around router registration converts a hard crash into silence, so "green" can mean "this area does not exist"** — the honest check is to import the modules and count the *mounted* surface on the running app, because a static gate cannot see a base class hidden behind `TYPE_CHECKING`, and a test-count delta cannot tell you 91 endpoints came back.
- Round 74 (authshield-lab-v3/-v5: **`GET /api/v1/quality/a11y/scorecards` raised `AttributeError` on every call, and 22 interface imports were missing from the module that serves it** `a78ec6e` v3 / `7ae9197` v5): finished the quality triage by pulling the defect-shaped mypy slice of `quality_routes.py` into focus. Because the module has `from __future__ import annotations`, the 22 **unresolvable repository interfaces in its annotations caused no runtime `NameError` at all** — they simply degraded every one of those assignments to unchecked `Any`, so neither the app nor a green test run could show the problem; mypy reporting 22 `name-defined` findings was the only evidence, and that is exactly the code that turns out to matter. One of them pointed at a genuine crash: `A11yScorecardRepository.find_all()` was **called but declared on neither the ABC nor the in-memory implementation**, so the public endpoint died with `AttributeError: 'InMemoryA11yScorecardRepository' object has no attribute 'find_all'` (HTTP 500). Fixed by declaring it on the interface, implementing it as `list(self._data.values())`, and importing all 22 interfaces in the route module. Added 3 tests to `test_repository_interface_contracts.py` driving the **real** in-memory repository and the **real** route handler (round 40's lesson: a mock fabricates any method, which is how this hid). **Proven non-vacuous: all 3 fail with the implementation removed.** Suite **912 passed** in each fork, the quality module's mypy findings cleared, the fleet-wide `name-defined` count dropped **43 → 21**, ruff lint **0** and existing format drift unchanged (`2 → 2`, so no churn on the 7 edited files). CI green: v3 `36978570661`, v5 `36978574253` + Security Scan `36978574281`. Lesson: with lazy annotations, **a missing import is invisible to the runtime** — the only surface that reports it is the type checker, which is an argument for keeping `mypy --strict` as a real gate and against treating its output as cosmetic.
- Round 72 (**first real-tag execution of radhika's `release` job — the whole tag→gate→build→publish path proven end-to-end, no manual step**): the user authorized cutting a real release to exercise the one surface a green `workflow_dispatch` cannot reach. Before doing so I checked the siblings and found **scan v1.2.27 (`36899629425`) and securepass v2.2.31 (`36896208491`) had already run `Publish GitHub Release -> success` on real tag pushes** — their generated bodies' `### Checksums (sha256)` blocks byte-match the live asset digests, so the pipeline was already proven for those two. The genuine remaining gap was **radhika**, whose `release` job never appeared in its v1.2.24 tag run (`36592258130` — v1.2.24 predates the job and was created by hand: no `SHA256SUMS.txt` asset, non-workflow body). Cut **radhika v1.2.25** from `6ec8277`: bumped pubspec `1.2.24+29`→`1.2.25+30` + `AppConstants.appVersion`, committed only those two files (`f6a3470` — the parallel session's uncommitted auth-screen work left untouched), pushed `main`, pushed tag `v1.2.25`. Tag run **`36980231041` SUCCESS, all three jobs** (`security-rules-tests`, `build-android`, `Publish GitHub Release`); the log shows the **pinned** `subosito/flutter-action@1a449444…` resolving and `Release signing configured.`; release `v1.2.25` is `draft=false` with assets AAB 67,304,491B + APK 71,082,129B + `SHA256SUMS.txt`, body generated from the real `git describe` changelog + Supported-Versions/Known-Limitations + checksums. **Independently verified from the published asset**: downloaded APK sha256 `79d72b2b…` == body == API digest, apksigner Signer #1 SHA-1 `7db5348e…` = anya-release-key-v2, aapt `versionName='1.2.25'`/`versionCode=42`. So the auto-release path now has a **real-tag** proof on all three apps and the 'only survives the real tag' class is fully closed. Current releases: radhika **v1.2.25**, scan **v1.2.27**, securepass **v2.2.31** (parallel session advanced scan/securepass past the round-68 table).
- Round 72 (**release-pipeline "only survives the real tag" surface audited clean — no code change**): after the artifact-path and release-notes bugs, I swept the remaining defect class a green `workflow_dispatch` cannot catch because it only executes on a real `v*` tag: (1) **token permissions** — all three `release` jobs declare job-level `permissions: contents: write` and the publish step sets `GH_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}`, so `gh release create` will **not** 403 the way it does when a repo's default `GITHUB_TOKEN` is read-only (the classic silent-until-tag failure); (2) **job deps + artifact wiring** — every `release: needs: build-android`, `build-android` uploads artifact `android-release`, and `release` downloads that same name into `build/`, so the download can neither race ahead of nor miss the upload; (3) **body/checksum generation** — the body's `### Checksums (sha256)` block is generated at release time from the just-computed `dist/SHA256SUMS.txt` (not hand-edited or stale), the collect step fails closed on a missing/ambiguous/`<1 MiB` asset, and the version-lie gate blocks a tag whose pubspec disagrees. Read from the committed workflows, not assumed. The only truly unexercised step left is GitHub executing that exact body on a real tag, which would create a real product release — deliberately left to the normal paste-a-commit cycle. Note: securepass `main` remains red at `fa41bfe` because the parallel session committed a scratch `test/zz_probe2_test.dart` (`avoid_print`) and has deleted it only in their working tree, not yet pushed.
- Round 72 (**closed the gate-verification asymmetry: radhika + scan `ci.yml` proven non-vacuous**): round 71 proved securepass's new `ci.yml` bites by pushing a canary to `main` and watching it go red — but radhika's and scan's gates had **only ever been green**, and my own recorded standard is that a gate which has never failed is indistinguishable from a no-op, so proving one of three identical workflows does not prove the other two. Rather than canary `main` again (the parallel session has uncommitted cookie-policy work in all three trees and periodic pushes, so a temporary red main risked a collision), I ran the canary on a **throwaway branch via `git worktree`**, leaving both the working trees and `main` completely untouched: branch `ci-gate-canary` + a one-line `final int ciGateCanaryBite = 'not an int';` in `lib/main.dart`, pushed, then `gh workflow run ci.yml --ref ci-gate-canary` (both `ci.yml`s carry `workflow_dispatch`, so no PR or `main` push was needed). **Both bit exactly right**: radhika run `36979173514` and scan run `36979178407` came back `failure` with `Analyze and test -> failure` and the precise `error • A value of type 'String' can't be assigned to a variable of type 'int' • lib/main.dart:54:30 • invalid_assignment` / `:48:30` (differs only because scan's `main.dart` is 6 lines shorter). Cleaned up fully — worktrees removed, local + remote `ci-gate-canary` deleted, 0 `ciGateCanaryBite` residue in any tracked tree, `main` still at `6ec8277`/`84e29bb`. All three shipping apps' push gates are now proven to bite, not merely green. Reusable technique: **canary a workflow on a worktree branch + `workflow_dispatch` instead of pushing to `main`** when another session is live in the tree.
- Round 72 (**third-party GitHub Actions in the keystore-bearing release job ran on mutable major tags — pinned to commit SHAs** `6ec8277` radhika / `84e29bb` scan / `fa41bfe` securepass): the secrets sweep's leftover question was whether an unset secret could reach a *hard* step, which raised the adjacent supply-chain risk — **the `build-android` job is the one holding `RELEASE_KEYSTORE_B64`, and it invoked `subosito/flutter-action@v2` + `r0adkll/upload-google-play@v1`**, both mutable tags, so a compromise of either action repo (or its maintainer's account) could retag `v2`/`v1` and run arbitrary code with the signing key in scope. Fleet numbers first: **183 action refs, 172 MAJORTAG, 9 SHA, 2 BRANCHISH (~95% unpinned)** — but mass-flagging 174 is the exact detector trap I had already hit three times, so I narrowed to the real surface: non-`actions/*` refs in the three apps is **11 refs / 4 actions / 6 files**, and only those in secret-holding jobs actually matter. Pinned each to the commit its tag resolves to today — **and dereferencing annotated tags was load-bearing: `r0adkll/upload-google-play@v1`, `Apple-Actions/upload-testflight-build@v1` and `softprops/action-gh-release@v{1,2,3}` are annotated tag objects, so `.object.sha` is the tag, not the commit; using it verbatim would pin to something Actions cannot run.** Final map: `subosito/flutter-action@v2→1a449444c387b1966244ae4d4f8c696479add0b2`, `r0adkll/upload-google-play@v1→e738b9dd8f2476ea806d921b64aacd24f34515a5`, `Apple-Actions/upload-testflight-build@v1→54dc215b4cd5529730db39f11c84efdb71414e07`, each keeping a trailing `# vN` comment for Dependabot-style bumps. **Verified rather than assumed**: `ci.yml` green on radhika `6ec8277` and scan `84e29bb`; all three `play-store-release.yml` off-tag dispatches green (`36904095444`/`36904104595`/`36904113676`), and the radhika log shows `Download action repository 'subosito/flutter-action@1a449444…'` plus `Configure release signing -> success` / `Release signing configured.` — so the pin resolves **and** the keystore still provisions. Two honest notes: (1) **securepass `ci.yml` is red on `fa41bfe`, but not from the pins** — it is the parallel session's accidentally-committed scratch `test/zz_probe2_test.dart` (`info • avoid_print • test/zz_probe2_test.dart:38:7`), which their own working tree has already deleted; my new gate is simply what surfaced it, and I left the file alone. (2) `actions/*` refs were deliberately **not** pinned: GitHub-owned, materially lower supply-chain risk, and pinning them all is churn the user has not asked for. Lesson: pin the **secret-holding third-party** surface first, and dereference annotated tags or the pin is a no-op.
- Round 73 (authshield-lab-v1/v3/v5: **every CI step had `continue-on-error: true`, so three of the four authshield repos reported green while being structurally incapable of failing** `60b1123` / `c568a3f` / `3aa75ff`): round 72's lesson ("a green run only proves the jobs that ran passed") pointed at the *next* unswept dimension — a gate that is green but **verifies nothing**, which is exactly the defect the parallel session's own round 71 had just found in the three shipping apps (workflows that run but never look at the app's code). Built a detector over every push/PR-gated workflow in all 29 repos (enumerate → parse YAML → count hard vs soft `run:` steps) and found **3 vacuous workflows**: `authshield-lab-v1/ci.yml` and `v3/ci.yml` had **all 12** steps soft, and `v5/ci.yml` had every Python verification soft (`ruff check`, `ruff format --check`, `mypy --strict`, unit, integration, `python -m build`) with only the `pip install` steps hard. In v1/v3 that meant **zero** hard verification of any kind. These are the repos where rounds 60-70 found live `AttributeError`/None-deref/`EventBus`/knowledge-graph crashes, so a green board was actively misleading. **Two hypotheses of mine were wrong before the real cause, and both are the kind that look right:** (1) a `grep`-based first pass flagged `betterleaks/test.yml` (a workflow literally named "test") and `byob/python-app.yml` as having zero verification steps — false positives, because both put their commands in multi-line `run: |` blocks that a single-line regex cannot see; reading them showed betterleaks genuinely runs `go build`, `gotestsum … go test ./... --race` and two config validations, and byob runs flake8 + pytest. (2) I assumed the forks' `ruff==0.2.2` pin was broken the way round 38 found the lab's was (that version *aborts* on an `UP042` selector) and that this is *why* the gates were soft — also wrong: the forks' `pyproject.toml` selects `"UP"` as a **prefix**, which every ruff version accepts, so 0.2.2 parses it fine. **The naive fix would have been catastrophic, and measuring first is what prevented it:** flipping lint/format to hard goes *massively* red on pre-existing debt — **1016** ruff errors + 62 unformatted files (v1), **5323** + 226 (v3), **5849** + 256 (v5) — and round 67 had already reverted exactly that class of change ("auto-fixed 2,580 pre-existing findings across 366 files — a diff I had no business shipping"). So the split is: **hard** the dependency installs and the **Python test gates**, which are green today (**514 / 909 / 909 passed**, measured with the lab venv, since the forks' own venvs lack pytest), and **leave soft** ruff/format/mypy plus all frontend/npm — with the measured debt written into the workflow as a comment so the remaining softness is a recorded decision rather than silent neglect (the lab already sets this precedent with its `check-defect-types.sh` ratchet for the same reason). Scope correction: I first hardened v5's `security` and `build` job installs too, then reverted that — round 38 hardened installs only in lint/typecheck/test-unit/test-integration and left build-job installs soft on purpose, so v5 now matches the lab's documented set exactly (asserted programmatically). Commits `60b1123`/`c568a3f`/`3aa75ff`, one workflow file each; all three runs **success** (`36902483718`, `36902488898`, `36902496546`). **Proven non-vacuous, which is the whole point of this round** — a green gate that has never gone red is indistinguishable from the no-op I just removed, so I canaried v1: pushed a deliberately failing `tests/unit/test_canary_gate_bite.py` (`58226d8`) and run `36902788125` came back **failure** with *exactly* the right signature — both `Test (3.11, 20)` and `Test (3.12, 20)` red while `Lint` and `Type Check` stayed green — and the log carries `tests/unit/test_canary_gate_bite.py::test_canary_gate_bites FAILED` and `1 failed, 514 passed`, which simultaneously re-confirms the 514 baseline. That identical failure was green before this change. Reverted as `b506b0b` → run `36903088294` **success**, canary file gone, 0 `canary_gate_bite` matches in `app/`+`tests/`, tree clean, and the step still parses as hard. Detector re-run afterwards: **0 vacuous workflows** fleet-wide, with coverage proven (**29** repos, **42** push/PR workflows containing run steps, **6** repos with no workflows) and validated in both directions — the 3 fixed repos moved 0→4/4/12 hard steps while `authshield-lab` (15 hard) and every other repo stayed non-vacuous. Left `authshield-lab` alone: its dirty `institution_service.py` and `28cea07` "use relative imports across collaboration and ecosystem modules" are the parallel session's round-70 follow-up on the 105 broken absolute imports, not mine. Lesson: **soft steps are not a style choice, they are an unrecorded claim that the gate passes** — and the reverse is equally true, because a *too*-hard gate invites the next person to re-soften it, so the debt has to be measured, recorded, and ratcheted rather than either hidden or demanded away in one 1000-error diff.
- Round 72 (**secrets-referenced-but-never-configured swept fleet-wide — 0 real defects, but only after fixing two bugs in my own detector**): audited all 29 repos for `secrets.X` referenced by a workflow with no matching secret, since an unset secret breaks a build only on the real tag. **The first run reported 64 of 64 missing, which is obviously a broken detector rather than 64 findings** — `GITHUB_TOKEN` is automatic, and radhika's `RELEASE_*` were added in round 20. Root cause: the response shape is `{"total_count":…,"secrets":[…]}`, so my `[.[].name]` iterated the *object's* values instead of `.secrets[].name`. **Second bug, subtler:** the corrected sweep found 22 apparent misses, and my gating heuristic only matched the literal `secrets.` inside an `if:`, so it missed gating on `env.`. With both fixed: **55 secret uses, 33 set, 22 referenced-but-unset — and every one of the 22 is correctly handled.** radhika's `Decode google-services.json` uses an inline `if [ -n "$GS_JSON" ]` with an explicit `else` (falling back to the committed file), same shape as the release-signing step; authshield-lab/-v5's `Sign commit with GPG` is gated `if: env.GPG_PRIVATE_KEY != ''` with the secret mapped through job-level `env:`, and the v1.0.2 run `36590974475` confirms both `Validate Release` and `Build Packages` green with that step skipped; and every Play Store / TestFlight upload is `continue-on-error: true`, which is correct by design since **Play upload is explicitly out of scope**. Worth stating plainly for future me: the unconfigured `PLAY_STORE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT`, `APPSTORE_*`, `DOCKER_*` and `HOMEBREW_TAP_TOKEN` mean **those uploads are permanently non-functional**, not merely currently broken — no AAB/AAB upload has ever happened and none is intended to until the user signs up for Play. Lesson, and it is the third time this session: **a detector that reports everything is broken, and a detector that reports nothing may be broken too** — the backtick sweep needed a coverage proof, the Dependabot counts needed two corroborating passes, and this one needed a positive control on radhika *plus* a manual read of the consuming step. A mass-reporting detector is a bug report about the detector.
- Round 72 (gp-crash-platform: **CI had been red on `main` since a security-fix commit — two test fixtures, not real secrets** `4e18b69`): the round-71 alert sweep was clean, but its own lesson ("a green run only proves the jobs that ran passed") pointed at the **unswept** dimension: CI *coverage*. Swept all 29 repos for *whether HEAD has a run at all* plus latest conclusion, and found **2 repos with a real failure on HEAD** — cipherforge-pro (the already-triaged round-70 `undici` Dependabot run; its lockfile resolves undici to 7.29.1 = `first_patched_version`, so Dependabot's resolver errors with nothing to fix; open alerts **0**, real `CI/CD Pipeline` + `Release` both **success** on HEAD `0f1e8e73` — no action) and **gp-crash-platform**, where run `36890934010` failed *only* the `secrets` job on `62b68449` ("fix(auth): atomic OTP attempt counter, and a refresh-token compare that was not constant-time") while the previous sha `f38a44a2` was green — i.e. **the repo's own security-fix commit turned its own security gate red and blocked every push since**. The log's decisive line was `leaks found: 2`. **Root cause is a false positive, and the values are provably not credentials:** both findings were in `tests/integration/auth-repo.test.ts` as `const SECRET = "integration-jwt-secret-0123456789"` and `passwordHash: "argon2-placeholder"`, tripped by `generic-api-key`. That job is deliberately load-bearing — its own comment says GitHub's secret scanning is unavailable on this repo's plan (private + free), *"so gitleaks scans the full history instead, which is the backstop that makes the gap tolerable rather than ignored"* — so the gate had to be made green **without weakening it**. Getting there took real digging, because the obvious config does not work: **`[[allowlists]]` (plural) is silently ignored.** Proved it rather than guessing: a match-all `regexes = ['''.*''']` still reported 2 findings, and a probe `targetRules = ['zzz-nonexistent-rule']` produced **no** error even though `config.go` must error *"target rule ID '%s' does not exist"* if the entry were parsed — so the section was never unmarshalled at all. The shipped `config/gitleaks.toml` shows why: it uses the **singular `[allowlist]`** (a *map*) carrying a literal `# TODO: change to [[allowlists]]`, so the plural array form is declared in the struct but not yet wired. With the singular form, `Validate()` ORs the regexes into `regexPat` and `RegexAllowed()` matches them against `finding.Secret` → `no leaks found`, exit 0. Because a global `[allowlist]` is a single map, both fixtures go in one entry, and I deliberately kept it to **only the 2 values that actually failed** (the two other `argon2id$v=19$…` fixtures in the same file never tripped the rule, so adding them would have been speculative breadth). **Proven non-vacuous in the right direction, and two first attempts were not:** injecting `AKIAIOSFODNN7EXAMPLE` appeared to prove the allowlist swallowed real secrets — it did not; that is AWS's *documented* key and gitleaks' **default** config already allowlists it, so it was never detected in either arm. The second attempt injected a high-entropy credential-shaped value but ran `gitleaks detect`, which scans **git history only** — the edit was uncommitted, so it was invisible in both arms. Redone with `gitleaks dir` (filesystem): baseline **41** findings → **39** with the allowlist, the exact 2 fixtures gone, and the injected `Zk7Qm2Xv9Lp4Rt8Wy3Nb6Hj1Cs5Df0Gu` **still reported in both** — the gate still bites on a real secret in the very file being allowlisted. (The other 39 are untracked `node_modules` noise that `dir` does not gitignore-filter; CI's git scan sees only the 2.) Final check used **CI's exact argv** (`detect --redact -v --exit-code=2 --report-format=sarif --log-level=debug --log-opts=-1`) → exit 0. Push was **rejected non-fast-forward** because a parallel session had just added `4438593` (a 291-line `tests/integration/realtime-socket.test.ts`) — rebased, re-ran gitleaks on the **new** base to confirm that file introduces no further findings, then pushed `4e18b69`. **CI run `36897607303` success, all 7 jobs green** including the previously-red `secrets`. **Fleet after: 25/25 workflow-bearing repos green on HEAD; the 4 remaining (Bluetooth-jammer-esp32, DetectionLab, LOIC, Provenance) are untouched upstream forks with `fork=true` and no workflows — no CI by design, consistent with round 19.** Lesson: a **false positive is still a red gate** — it blocks every subsequent push and trains people to ignore or disable security jobs, so the fix belongs in a narrowly scoped allowlist verified to still catch a real secret, never in skipping the job; and when a config knob appears inert, prove it inert (match-all control + a parse-failure probe) before concluding the *content* is wrong, because the *schema* was the bug.
- Round 71 follow-up (**push-CI coverage audited across all 29 repos — the three apps were the last gap in anything I maintain**): trigger-audited every `.github/workflows/*.yml` in every repo by its `on:` block. Of **54 workflow files**: 33 `PUSH-DEFAULT`, 10 `TAGS-ONLY`, 4 `NO-PUSH`, 1 `PUSH-OTHER`, 6 repos with no workflows at all. **7 repos have no push-triggered CI** — Bluetooth-jammer-esp32, ReconRover, Provenance, LOIC, darkdump, malware_showcase (no workflows) and DetectionLab (workflows but no `push` trigger) — and I checked rather than assumed: **all 7 are `fork=true`**, several untouched since 2024 (LOIC 2024-01, DetectionLab 2024-07, Bluetooth-jammer 2024-11). That is consistent with the standing round-19/round-55 decision to leave upstream research/security-tool forks alone, so **no action**. The load-bearing result is the other side: **all 10 repos I actively maintain now have `PUSH-DEFAULT` CI** — radhika, scan-translate-ai, securepass-pro, cipherforge-pro, chevvy, authshield-lab{,-v1,-v3,-v5} and securescan-pro-x — so the Dart-CI gap just closed really was the last of its kind, and a broken `main` anywhere in active work is now caught by a job that runs on the push. Same lesson as the backtick sweep: a zero is only worth reporting once the coverage and the detector are both proven.
- Round 71 (**none of the three shipping apps ran `flutter analyze` or `flutter test` on any push to `main`** `3c2b425` / `e65d3b4` / `38e3930`): after the backtick bug shipped *into a release job that had never executed*, the deeper question was why nothing upstream had caught it. Trigger audit of every workflow in the three shipping repos: **securepass-pro had exactly one workflow, `play-store-release.yml`, which is push-on-`v*`-tags only** — so `main` carried **zero** automated verification, and radhika/scan's only push-triggered workflow is `firebase-deploy.yml`, which runs the Firestore emulator rules suite and **never looks at Dart code at all**. So a commit that breaks the build or the test suite can merge to any of the three `main`s and ship in the next tag with nothing red anywhere. That is the same systemic hole the securescan Round-71 entry describes, and it is exactly how the release-notes bug reached a shipping app. Added `.github/workflows/ci.yml` to **all three**: `push: [main]` + `pull_request` + `workflow_dispatch`, steps `flutter pub get` → `flutter analyze lib/ test/` → `flutter test`, and deliberately **strict** (no `continue-on-error` anywhere), reusing the repo's existing conventions verbatim (`actions/checkout@v4`, `subosito/flutter-action@v2` pinned to `flutter-version: 3.47.3`, `cache: true`) rather than inventing a new toolchain. **Baselines verified locally *before* wiring, so the gate could never be born red**: securepass 0 issues + **75/75** (AGENTS previously said 73/73 — drifted by 2, recorded), radhika 0 + 12/12, scan 0 + 21/21. All three runs green — `36896919330` (securepass `3c2b425`), `36897132931` (radhika `e65d3b4`), `36897141824` (scan `38e3930`). **Proven non-vacuous, which is the only part that matters**: a gate that has never gone red is indistinguishable from a no-op, so I pushed a deliberate `final int gateCanaryBite = 'not an int';` into securepass `lib/main.dart` (local analyze reproduced it as `error • A value of type 'String' can't be assigned to a variable of type 'int' … invalid_assignment`), confirmed CI on `8dedd5b` came back **failure** carrying that exact analyzer line, then `git revert`ed as `4e5217d` and confirmed green again with `lib/main.dart` **byte-identical to its pre-canary state** (`cmp` clean) and zero `gateCanaryBite` occurrences. Canary fully removed — no residue on any branch. Also **swept the backtick bug fleet-wide** rather than assuming the other 26 repos were clean: enumerated all 29 repos, resolved every `.github/workflows/*.yml` via the git tree API, and checked every double-quoted `echo` line in every `run:` block for unescaped backticks and `$<digit>` positional-parameter expansion. **Coverage proven, not assumed — 45 workflow files across 23 repos** (6 repos have no workflows at all), **0 instances at HEAD**, and the detector was proved non-vacuous by firing exactly 2× on the known-bad *pre-fix* securepass file at `d3fe67e`. So that class is now fleet-wide fixed, and the same reasoning that found one instance in the first place is what justifies trusting the zero. **Coordination note:** scan-translate-ai's working tree holds a **parallel session's uncommitted work** (2 modified pages + a new `empty_input_validation_test.dart`); I staged only my own workflow file (`git show --name-only` confirms the commit is workflow-only) and left their files untouched — but they should expect the new gate to verify their work, and to go red if it lands mid-edit.
- Round 71 (securescan-pro-x: **a Dependabot commit had silently broken `main`'s build and re-opened 7 alerts — repaired before it could ship** `de0ea14`): the three shipping apps were clean, so I swept the wider org (29 repos — it is a **user account, not an org**, so `orgs/…/repos` 404s and `user/repos?affiliation=owner` is the correct endpoint) and found **7 open Dependabot alerts on securescan-pro-x**, a repo the fleet notes had at 0 since round 55#6. Root cause was not a missed fix but a **regression**: Dependabot commit `f5805a9` ("bump @tauri-apps/api 2.11.1→2.12.0") was built from a stale base and, instead of touching one line, **overwrote the entire dependency block with an older `package.json`** — dropping `zustand` and `clsx` (both still imported by 4 source files, incl. `src/stores/index.ts`'s `import { create } from "zustand"`), dropping `@types/node` (which `tsconfig` references as a type library), downgrading `@types/react`/`@types/react-dom` **19→18** against `react@19`, and reverting the security floors `vite ^6.0.0→^5.4.8` + `vitest ^4.1.11→^2.1.1`. **Proven, not inferred**: after `npm ci`, `require.resolve('zustand')` returned UNRESOLVED, `tsc -b` exited 1 with `TS2688 Cannot find type definition file for 'node'` plus 16 cascading `TS7006`s, and `npm audit` reported 5 findings (1 critical, 1 high) — matching the 7 alerts (alerts > audit findings because several advisories map to the same installed copy). So `main` had a **broken build** (`stores/index.ts` could not compile) sitting behind green Dependabot-only jobs, and **no CI run existed on that sha at all** to catch it. **The 4 open Dependabot PRs (#15–#18) could not have fixed it** — all were based on the broken `f5805a9` and each only bumps vite/vitest/esbuild/`@vitest/mocker`, none restores the removed runtime deps. Fix: restore `frontend/package.json` + `frontend/package-lock.json` to the last known-good tree `72bacfb` (which resolved vite 6.4.3 / vitest 4.1.11 / esbuild 0.25.12 — all at/past `patched` — plus zustand 5.0.15, clsx 2.1.1, @types/node 22.20.4), deliberately reverting the legitimate `@tauri-apps/api` semver-minor along with the damage so it can be re-proposed on a clean base. Confirmed safe first: the bot's one *addition*, `recharts`, is imported by **no** source file. **Full gate re-run on the restored tree**: `npm audit` **0 vulnerabilities**, `tsc -b` exit 0, `vite build` exit 0 (61 modules), `vitest` **3/3**, `eslint --max-warnings 0` exit 0. Pushed `f5805a9..de0ea14`; CI run `36892229826` **success** and all 4 Dependabot update runs success; **alerts 7 → 0** with no empty commit or forced rescan. The 4 stale PRs auto-closed; only PR **#19** (vitest 4.1.11→**5.0.3**, a major) remains, user-gated. **Fleet re-verified: 0 open alerts across all 29 repos.** Lesson, in the same family as the radhika/securepass stale-asset releases: **a green run proves only that the jobs that ran passed — a commit can be entirely untested by CI and still be merged.** Always resolve the installed dependency set (`require.resolve` / `tsc` / `npm audit`) rather than reading the manifest, and check *which* shas actually have a CI run before trusting a repo's green status.
- Round 70 (securepass: **backticks in the release notes silently deleted the keystore name — the first real tag would have published "signed with the project release key ()"** `c43c780`): having made the release job's asset paths layout-independent, I simulated **the entire job body in order** — every `run:` block extracted from the committed workflow and executed against a real `git clone` with real tags — because the job had still never run on GitHub and each remaining step was unproven. **The simulation immediately found a second real bug that a green run would have hidden**: the securepass notes step ends with `echo "- Release builds are signed with the project release key (\`anya-release-key-v2\`)."`, and **backticks inside a double-quoted string are a command substitution in bash**. Running it verbatim printed `anya-release-key-v2: command not found` to stderr, `echo` still exited 0, and the published line silently lost the key name entirely. So the step *succeeds* — the failure is invisible in CI output except as one stderr line — and securepass's first automated release would have shipped a Known Limitations entry reading `Release builds are signed with the project release key ().` Fix: escape the backticks. Verified by re-running the step against a real clone at the real `v2.2.30` tag: **stderr now byte-empty**, and the line reads `...release key (\`anya-release-key-v2\`).` **Then CI-proven**: securepass has *no* push-triggered workflow (release is tag-only), so the commit had zero checks of its own — a dispatch probe `36893866058` on `c43c780` returned success (`build-android` ✓, `build-ios` ✓, `Publish GitHub Release: skipped`, correctly gated off-tag), which also proves the edited file is structurally valid on GitHub (a malformed workflow would not start at all). A scripted sweep of every double-quoted `echo` line in all three release jobs for `` ` `` and `$(` hazards found this **one** occurrence — radhika and scan are clean. This is the same family as the round-65/66 releases that were "green" and unusable: a step can pass while its output is wrong, so the only real check is asserting the *rendered text*, not the exit code. **Full-job simulation now green for all three repos**: radhika and scan verified step-by-step (gate → collect → notes → publish) on real clones at their real tags, including the `gh release create` argv (`release create <tag> --title <tag> --notes-file NOTES.md dist/app-release.aab dist/app-release.apk dist/SHA256SUMS.txt`) and both notes branches (previous tag present → real changelog from `git describe`; orphan tag → `- Initial tagged release.` + a release-tag link). What remains unexercised is only GitHub itself executing that body on a real tag, which would create a real product release.
- Round 70 (radhika + scan + securepass: **the auto-release job added earlier this round could never have published anything — fixed before it could ship a broken release** `0fedf2b` / `516b9cb` / `d3fe67e`): the new `release` job had only ever run as `skipped` (on a `workflow_dispatch`), so **nothing had ever executed its body**, and a green dispatch proves nothing about a job that never runs — the same lesson as rounds 65/66. Auditing it before the first real tag found a **latent, guaranteed failure**: the collect step hardcoded `cp build/app/outputs/bundle/release/app-release.aab` and `.../flutter-apk/app-release.apk`, but `actions/upload-artifact@v4` stores each file **relative to the least common ancestor of the uploaded paths**, so the artifact contains `bundle/release/app-release.aab` + `flutter-apk/app-release.apk` and `download-artifact` yields `build/bundle/...` / `build/flutter-apk/...` — neither hardcoded path exists. **Proven against a real artifact, not assumed**: instead of a 100 MB download I read the **ZIP central directory** of artifact `11173832563` by fetching only the last 200 KB of the signed blob URL with a single `curl --range`, then scanning for `PK\x01\x02` entries — which returned exactly those two paths. Under `set -euo pipefail` both `cp`s fail, so the first real tag would have built and signed happily and then **published no release at all**. Fix makes the step layout-independent: `find` the two assets by name, require **exactly one** of each, reject anything under 1 MiB (a truncated upload still passes `sha256sum -c`), then checksum. **Proven in both directions**: 5-case harness — real layout → exit 0 with the 3 assets + a verifiable `SHA256SUMS.txt`; empty dir, 5 KB truncated AAB, two AABs present, and APK-only all fail loudly with distinct `::error::` messages; and the **old** script re-run against the real layout still fails, confirming the bug was real rather than theoretical. Commits `0fedf2b` (radhika) / `516b9cb` (scan) / `d3fe67e` (securepass), one workflow file each. **Then CI-proven**: radhika dispatch `36890676440` on the fixed sha → `security-rules-tests: success` + `build-android: success` + `Publish GitHub Release: skipped` (correctly skipped off-tag), so the patched job is valid on GitHub and the build is unaffected. The version gate was re-verified in 4 cases (match passes, mismatch blocks, quoted version passes, quoted `+build` passes). Two reusable techniques worth keeping: **read an Actions artifact's file list from its ZIP central directory via one HTTP range request** instead of downloading 100–210 MB, and **always re-count a fleet-wide Dependabot total** — see below.

- Round 70 (Dependabot fleet counts are **racy — never act on a single sweep**): chasing the 7 open alerts that briefly appeared in a fleet-wide count, securescan-pro-x showed `vitest`/`vite`/`esbuild` alerts against `frontend/package-lock.json`, every one with a patched version — and every one a **false positive against the current tree**: the committed lockfile resolves a single copy of each to `vite 6.4.3`, `vitest 4.1.11`, `esbuild 0.25.12`, `@vitest/mocker 4.1.11` (all at/past `patched`), and `npm audit` for that manifest reports **0 findings**. The alerts had been *created and closed inside the measurement window*: Dependabot commit `f5805a9` briefly reverted the frontend manifest, then `de0ea14` ("restore frontend manifest reverted by Dependabot commit f5805a9") restored it 19 minutes later, so my count read 0 → 7 → 0. Re-counting twice 20 s apart settled it at **0 open, 9 fixed**. Two traps avoided: a `node` one-liner that `break`s after the first lockfile match would have hidden any nested duplicate copy (listing **all** copies of each package is required), and a single point-in-time fleet total is not evidence of anything. Same family as round 55's cipherforge `undici` stale alert — **corroborate any alert with the resolved version and `npm audit` before trying to "fix" it.** Recorded: fleet is at **0 open alerts**, verified on two independent passes.
- Round 70 (authshield-lab: **733 of 785 API routes were mounted at a doubled path** `49a7d8c`): porting round 69's defect-type gate to the sibling forks surfaced a code mypy had never been asked about — the forks' own CI invokes `mypy app/ --ignore-missing-imports` (**not** `--strict`), and under that invocation v3 reports **43 `[name-defined]`**, a code absent from the lab's strict view and absent from my gate's code list. Chasing it: `app/collaboration/services/research_service.py` does `from domain.entities.research_workspace import ResearchProject` — an **absolute** import of a package that does not exist (the real path is `app.collaboration.domain…`), so `create_project(...)` raises `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'domain'` when actually called. Proven against the real service with a stub repo. Scope: **105 such broken absolute-import sites in the lab, 107 each in v3/v5** (v1 has none — it lacks the ecosystem/collaboration modules). That is dead-but-present code, not yet a startup crash, because every router import in `app/api/v1/router.py` is wrapped in `try: … except (ImportError, AttributeError): pass`. While proving reachability I asked what the *live* app object actually exposes and found the far bigger defect: **`api_v1_router` is `APIRouter(prefix="/api/v1")` and thirteen sub-routers ALSO declared `prefix="/api/v1/<area>"`**, so FastAPI concatenated them. Measured on the running app before the fix: **785 routes total, 733 at `/api/v1/api/v1/…`, only 52 correct** — the entire LMS / simulation / content-studio / standards / certification / developer / production / quality / analytics / optimization surface was unreachable at its documented path, a 404 for any client that had not noticed the doubling. The routers that got it right (authentication, users, sessions, audit) declare `prefix="/auth"` with no version segment, and the frontend only ever calls `/api/v1/auth/*`, so **nothing depended on the doubled form** and the fix is purely additive reachability. After: 785 routes, **0 doubled**, all 785 correct. Fix is one token per file (13 files) — no route path, handler, or response model changed, only where they mount. New `tests/unit/test_api_route_prefixes.py` (13) asserts no route sits at `/api/v1/api/v1`, that the surface is still large (guards against "fixing" it by dropping `include_router`), that ten areas are reachable under their documented prefixes, and that auth/users are unchanged; **proven non-vacuous** (restoring one doubled prefix fails 2). Unit 933 (+13), integration 79, ruff clean. **The new gate caught my own regression here** — `attr-defined: 2 -> 3` from reading `.path` off a `BaseRoute` (only `APIRoute` has it) in the new test — which I fixed rather than loosening the baseline. CI all 7 jobs success. Two follow-ups deliberately not done yet: the 105–107 broken absolute imports, and `name-defined` (a genuine `NameError` class) added to the gate's code list.

- Round 70 (radhika + scan: **two live HIGH-severity `@grpc/grpc-js` alerts closed with an npm override — fleet is now at 0 open alerts** `0f46637` radhika / `561523c` scan): chasing the two failed `Dependabot Updates` runs (`36883907815` radhika, `36884025075` scan) rather than writing them off as the round-55 "unfixable upstream" class **surfaced a real live finding**: both runs were *security* update jobs for `@grpc/grpc-js`, and the log's `Failed to parse GITHUB_REGISTRIES_PROXY` is a red herring noise line — the jobs failed because Dependabot's own resolver died and never opened a PR, leaving **2 genuinely open alerts on each repo** (`GHSA-m9gg-hp2v-232j` high + `GHSA-f596-whhp-79r4` low), both with `first_patched_version = 1.13.6`, i.e. **fixable, not a floor**. Reading the lockfile rather than the manifest found the exact shape: there are five `@grpc/grpc-js` copies in `firebase/rules-testing` and four are already at a patched **1.14.5** (via `google-gax`); the single vulnerable one is the *top-level* `1.9.16`, held there by `@firebase/firestore` declaring **`~1.9.0`** — a tilde range no automatic updater can move, which is precisely why Dependabot errored instead of proposing a fix. Fixed with `"@grpc/grpc-js": "1.14.5"` in the **`overrides`** block the manifest already carried for `uuid@<11.1.1` (radhika), so this follows the repo's existing convention rather than inventing one. **Proven, not assumed**: `npm audit` for that manifest went from **8 findings (5 high, 3 moderate) → 3, all moderate** — the override cleared the grpc-js pair *and* the 4 cascading high entries (`@firebase/firestore`, `-compat`, `@firebase/rules-unit-testing`, `firebase`), with the residue being only `firebase-tools`' own `@opentelemetry/core` baggage issue; the real Firestore+Storage emulator suites still pass **10/10** and **17/17** locally (`JAVA_HOME=/home/anya/tools/jdk21`), so forcing a 5-minor jump past a pinned tilde range provably does not change emulator behaviour. **Then CI-proven rather than trusted**: `firebase-deploy.yml` gates `deploy-rules: needs: security-rules-tests`, so the passing gate proves the suite ran green on the pushed sha — runs `36887138367` (radhika) and `36887163292` (scan) both show `security-rules-tests: success` + `deploy-rules: success`. GitHub's own rescan confirms it: both alerts flipped to **`state=fixed`** (`fixed_at 15:48:38Z` / `15:48:53Z`) with **no** empty commit or forced rescan needed. Scope is dev-only (`dependencies: {}`; all three are devDeps) so nothing about the shipped APK changed — no version bump, no tag, no release. **Second, separate finding — a stale alert, deliberately NOT "fixed"**: the fleet sweep then showed exactly one open alert left org-wide, `cipherforge-pro` `GHSA-w293-vg96-wgc3` (`undici`, **high**, "TLS certificate validation bypass via dropped connect options in BalancedPool"). It is a **false positive against the current tree**: the lockfile resolves the single `undici` copy to **7.29.1**, which *is* `first_patched_version` and sits **outside** the vulnerable range `>= 7.24.1, < 7.29.1`, and commit `09e413d` had already bumped it from 7.28.0 on **2026-09-28** — three days *before* the alert was created at `2026-10-01T08:41:02Z`, so it was computed from a stale pre-bump graph snapshot. I confirmed `npm install --package-lock-only` returns a **byte-identical** lockfile, so there is no honest change to push to force re-evaluation. Dismissed as `inaccurate` (not `not_used` — the package genuinely is used, it just isn't a vulnerable version) with the evidence in the comment, which round 55's 280-char cap required trimming to 230. **FINAL FLEET STATE: 28 repos, 0 open Dependabot alerts**, verified by looping every repo and summing. Lesson carried forward: **a failed Dependabot *security* job is an alert triage signal, not a resolution** — round 55 correctly dismissed genuinely-unfixable ones as `not_used`, but this pair was quietly fixable the whole time, and the only reason it surfaced was refusing to accept the first plausible explanation for the failure. Corollary: an open alert whose installed version already equals `first_patched_version` is stale server-side state, and `patched=7.29.1` on a package sitting at exactly 7.29.1 is the tell.

- Round 69 (authshield-lab: **a hard CI gate on the defect-shaped slice of mypy, so this class of crash cannot return** `816323d` + `39819e4`): round 68 left the family with all defect-shaped mypy findings triaged and `mypy --strict` still a *soft* step everywhere — which is a real gap, because the defect-shaped codes are exactly what produced every live crash found by static triage in the last several rounds (repository methods missing from their ABCs, four None-dereferences in `publishing_center_service`, a wrong kwarg name that crashed a validator, an `EventBus` except-block that raised while handling a handler failure, a knowledge graph that silently dropped prerequisite edges). Those are cheap to fix when written and expensive to find later, and nothing stopped any of them coming back. Added `scripts/utilities/check-defect-types.sh` as a **ratchet**: it records the current per-code counts in `backend/mypy-defect-baseline.txt` and fails only if any of them *increases*, wired in as a **hard** step (`continue-on-error` deliberately not set) immediately after the still-soft full `mypy --strict` run in the existing Type Check job, so it reuses that job's already-installed mypy. This deliberately respects round 38's rationale — the thousands of `type-arg` / `no-untyped-def` / `no-any-return` annotation errors stay soft and can only ever go down, never up. **Verified the gate actually bites rather than passing vacuously**: unchanged tree → PASS (93 == baseline 93); restoring the `min_length`/`min_len` wrong-kwarg regression → **FAIL, exit 1, "call-arg: 24 -> 26"**; restored → PASS. CI green on all four repos. Two things worth recording honestly. (1) **The gate cannot catch the round-63 EventBus bug** — that was an annotation *lie* (`event_type` is declared `EventType` but every subclass declares a plain `str`), and mypy has no way to see it; I proved this by injecting `_et.value` back into `event_bus.py` and watching the gate pass. It covers the detectable subset, which is still the majority of what static triage has found. (2) The first CI run reported **90** findings against the committed **93**, because mypy under Python 3.12 yields a few fewer `arg-type`/`attr-defined` results than 3.11 (`arg-type` 43 vs 44, `attr-defined` 1 vs 2 — a stdlib-stub difference, not a code change). The per-code comparison already tolerated that, but the script rewrote the baseline file on any decrease, which made the checked-in value depend on which interpreter ran — and CI cannot commit it anyway. Changed so a decrease passes and leaves the recorded maximum in place (`39819e4`), then re-verified all three paths.
- Round 68 (authshield family: **v1's copy of the stdlib-logger crash was LIVE, not latent — fixed** `0c89fd8`): round 67 fixed the stdlib-logger class in lab/v3/v5 and correctly noted that in those repos the landmine was **armed but not lit** — `main.py` only *imported* `setup_logging`. I did not check whether v1 was the same, and it was not. **v1 calls it**: `app/main.py:32`, inside the FastAPI `lifespan` startup handler, `setup_logging(log_level=settings.app.log_level.value)` — and `log_level` defaults to `LogLevel.DEBUG` (`app/config/settings.py:59`). So v1's root logger sits at DEBUG from the first request, which makes CPython's `Logger.info()` actually reach `self._log(INFO, msg, args, **kwargs)` and raise. Proven end-to-end against v1's own startup sequence: `setup_logging(log_level="DEBUG")` then the exact call from `permission_registry` → `*** TypeError: Logger._log() got an unexpected keyword argument 'name'`. So this was a **live crash on the first log line**, not a landmine. Fixed by migrating v1's six affected modules to the same `get_logger()` structlog helper the other three now use — 14 insertions / 12 deletions, no call site touched, and the same call now emits `permission_overwritten  name=users.read old_version=1` at DEBUG. mypy `--strict`: all 53 `[call-arg]` findings in v1 are gone (0 across all four repos). Unit 454 + integration 60 unchanged. **Two self-inflicted slips caught and corrected**: (1) my import insertion left no blank line before the relative-import group in two of v1's files, adding 2 `I001` findings (77 vs 75 baseline) — fixed by spacing them, back to exactly 75 (the remaining `F401` on `permission_registry`'s unused `dataclasses.field` import is pre-existing, verified against `git show HEAD:`); (2) a `git stash` during a before/after lint measurement collided with a stale `.git/index.lock` and failed to pop — I verified the tree, removed the lock, and restored the work from `stash@{0}` before continuing. **CI green on all four repos.**

- Round 68 (radhika v1.2.24 + scan v1.2.26 **"About lies" versions fixed and shipped in-place**): a routine fleet/CI sweep found the shipping repos had advanced beyond the AGENTS table (radhika → v1.2.24, scan → v1.2.26 — round-53 privacy-consent model applied and released by a parallel session, all CI green). **Auditing the NEW releases surfaced the round-49/50 trap live in both**: the parallel session cut each tag BEFORE the version bump landed, and their `play-store-release.yml` passes only `--build-number` (versionCode) — never `--build-name` — so versionName comes straight from pubspec. radhika's released "v1.2.24" APK reported `versionName 1.2.23 / versionCode 37`, scan's released "v1.2.26" reported `1.2.25 / 38`; both tags AND both repo mains still carried the stale pubspec (`1.2.23+28` / `1.2.25+30`) AND stale `AppConstants.appVersion` fallbacks (`'1.2.23'` / `'1.2.25'`). **Fixed exactly as round 50 did** — bump pubspec + appVersion same commit, move the tag, rebuild, verify, clobber-replace: radhika committed `5c37311` on the parallel `feature/privacy-policy-gate` branch first, then **cherry-picked onto main as `dbd03a3` and `git branch -f` the feature branch back to its origin state `9a5b009`** (zero disturbance of the parallel owner's branch) → tag v1.2.24 force-moved `9a5b009→dbd03a3` → run `36592258130` success (security-rules-tests gate + build-android) → artifact 11044213810 → **AAB `b212eae5…` (67,304,458 B) / APK `fd01df0d…` (71,082,125 B)** → `aapt` badging **`versionName=1.2.24 versionCode=38`**, apksigner SHA-1 `7db5348e…` = anya-release-key-v2, `gh release upload --clobber` on release 398442982 → API digests match local exactly. scan: same bump committed directly on main as `039b2e2` (`1.2.26+31` + appVersion `'1.2.26'`) → tag v1.2.26 force-moved `9d38261→039b2e2` → run `36595398589` success (build-android ✓, security-rules-tests ✓, build-ios = the usual tolerated pod failure) → artifact 11045434779 → **AAB `8f9a7fb3…` (129,408,584 B) / APK `ce6853c2…` (163,341,512 B)** → **`versionName=1.2.26 versionCode=39`**, release-signed, `--clobber`-replaced on release 398443509 → published bytes downloaded and hashed equal to local (both assets `uploaded`, API digests match, both URLs 302→200). **Both release bodies already documented the correction correctly** (written to match the intended state, incl. a "Version / replaces the earlier build" note + Checksums that byte-match — the only gap was the live assets themselves). **Gotchas this round:** (a) `gh release upload --clobber` can leave assets in `state=starter` (the old ones deleted, replacement NOT landed, and the release-download URL 404s) — after upload, **verify `state=uploaded` and curl the actual `releases/download/` URL (+ hash it)**, never trust silent success or the digest field; (b) `releases/tags/<tag>` and the `/releases?per_page` collection showed `assets=0`/`digest=null` while the per-asset listing confirmed the files — use the assets endpoint; (c) `dl.sh`'s cap loop had been frozen at exactly 200 MiB on a 210.7 MiB artifact for ~40 min and the `$5` log arg I passed is **not used by the script** (its stdout went to /dev/null under setsid, so the log was always empty) — diagnosed by hand, minted one fresh 302, fetched the `--range 209715200-` tail directly (206, 1,009,818 B) and appended; (d) radhika artifact layout is `bundle/release/app-release.aab` + `flutter-apk/app-release.apk`, not a bare `*.aab` at root. Cleanup done: stray zips (`radhika_v1.2.24.fixed.zip`, `scan_v1.2.26.fixed.zip`) and `/tmp` extracts removed. Suites: both apps analyze/test baselines unchanged (12/12 radhika, 21/21 scan); no source changes — version identity only.
