Table updated: radhika v1.2.24, scan v1.2.26 (both now genuinely matching their tags). **Follow-up verification**: ran the same independent end-to-end pass on the remaining shipping releases — securepass v2.2.30 downloaded APK badging `versionName='2.2.30' versionCode='39'`, release-signed `7db5348e…`, AAB `4e0ecdbf…`/APK `c5f5e327…` byte-match its body; and for all three Flutter releases the **release-body checksum block == live API `digest`** (`b212eae5…`/`fd01df0d…`, `8f9a7fb3…`/`ce6853c2…`, `4e0ecdbf…`/`c5f5e327…`), closing the readiness `--releases` verification loop (body ↔ API ↔ downloaded bytes). cipherforge v1.0.2 verified to have no user-visible version (not a lie); authshield-lab v1.0.2 backend+frontend both `1.0.2`. Fleet-wide version-identity audit complete — every shipping repo's release now genuinely matches its tag.

- Round 67 (authshield family: **stdlib loggers were one INFO level away from crashing 91 log calls** `a51b9b3` lab / `be6eff6` v3 / `e1d01bb` v5): re-examined the largest remaining defect-shaped class — the 91 `[call-arg]` findings in the lab, which rounds 58 and 63 had recorded as "false positives: stdlib `logging` accepts arbitrary `**kwargs`, mypy just resolves to structlog's stricter `Logger`". **That classification was wrong, and the way it was wrong is the interesting part.** CPython's `Logger.info()` is `def info(self, msg, *args, **kwargs): if self.isEnabledFor(INFO): self._log(INFO, msg, args, **kwargs)` — the arbitrary kwargs are only forwarded to `_log()` when INFO is actually enabled. Verified both ways against the real stdlib: level not enabled → the call returns and the kwargs are silently discarded; level enabled → `TypeError: Logger._log() got an unexpected keyword argument 'name'`. **That is exactly why a 911-test suite never caught it, and why my earlier empirical spot-check came back clean** — the root logger sits at WARNING by default, so all 91 calls were silent no-ops. The landmine was armed but not lit. It is one line from lit: `app/main.py` already imports `setup_logging`, which defaults to `log_level="DEBUG"` and calls `root.setLevel(DEBUG)`; any future `setup_logging()` call, or any deployment that turns INFO on, turns all 91 sites into `TypeError`s on the first log line. It was also a **silent data-loss bug even in the no-op case** — the code clearly intends structured logging (`logger.info("permission_overwritten", name=..., old_version=..., new_version=...)`) and stdlib logging was dropping every one of those fields. **Lesson sharpened: "runtime-correct" is only correct for the configuration you happened to test.** A defect gated behind a log level, a feature flag, or an env var survives any amount of green-suite evidence. Fixed by migrating the **8** affected modules to the codebase's existing `get_logger()` helper, which returns a `structlog.stdlib.BoundLogger` that takes those kwargs as event context — the same pattern their sibling modules (e.g. `production/services/knowledge_service.py`) already use. Now `logger.info("permission_overwritten", name="users.read", old_version="1", new_version="2")` emits `[info] permission_overwritten  name=users.read old_version=1 new_version=2`. **18 insertions / 16 deletions across 8 files; not one call site was touched.** mypy `--strict`: all 91 `[call-arg]` findings in these three repos are gone. **Self-inflicted churn caught and reverted**: I first ran `ruff check --fix app/` to tidy the import placement, which in v3/v5 auto-fixed **2,580 pre-existing findings across 366 files** — a diff I had no business shipping. Reverted both repos with `git checkout -- backend/` and redid the import placement in the migration script itself (inserting into the relative-import group where isort wants it), so the diff is exactly 8 files. Unit 920/849/849 and integration 79/60/60 unchanged; format-drift files in the forks identical before and after (6 pre-existing unformatted either way). **CI green on all four repos.**

- Round 66 (authshield family: **v3/v5 + v1 residual defect-shaped triage closed out; `_random_choice` type-contract lie fixed** `620de49` lab / `109e05d` v3 / `9ff0281` v5): round 64 left one class I had never examined in the forks, and I nearly repeated round 60's mistake by asserting the remainder was benign without checking — so I enumerated every remaining defect-shaped class and classified each one on evidence. **v1's `[operator]` (2) and `[override]` (2) were carried over as "pre-existing" for several rounds but had never actually been examined in that repo**: the 2 `[operator]` sites are `count_result.scalar() > 0` in `role_repository.py` and both already carry an explicit `# type: ignore[union-attr]`, and a `count()` query always yields an int, so they are benign. The 2 `[override]` are `search` signature variance against `BaseRepository` (typing wart), the 1 `[assignment]` is `password_hasher.py:165` where an inner `dict[str, object]` is inferred from mixed str/bool values against a declared `dict[str, Any]` (runtime-correct), and the 1 remaining `event_type.value` is in `authentication/events/event_publisher.py:60` — the *same fragile shape* as the round-63 bus bug, but **unreachable**: the parameter is annotated `EventType` and an AST sweep of every `app/` call site found zero string literals passed as `event_type`. The residual `[arg-type]` after the round-63/64 fixes are the 18 `_publish_event` sites (already made safe by the bus fix) plus 5 that are all benign — `ScenarioDifficulty`/`ScenarioType` are `str, Enum` so a raw string compares equal to the member, `production_event_handlers` subscribes with a `str` which is the same key space the bus uses, and `main.py`'s `add_middleware` is a known Starlette variance wart. **One genuine defect did remain, in the class I had never opened: `[list-item]` (10 per repo).** `dataset_generator.py`'s `_random_choice` was declared `(items: list[str]) -> str`, but 2 of its 32 call sites pass a *list of lists* — the assistive-technology column (`[["NVDA"], ["JAWS", "NVDA"], ["VoiceOver"]]`) and the compliance-framework column (`[["SOC2"], ["NIST", "SOC2"]]`) — so the helper returned a `list[str]` where it promised a `str`, and mypy flagged all ten list items against the flattened literal. The value flows into a JSON-serialized synthetic dataset artifact, so it was **correct at runtime**; the annotation was simply misdescribing it. Made the helper generic over its item type (`_T = TypeVar("_T")`, `def _random_choice(self, items: list[_T]) -> _T`), which is what `self._rng.choice(items)` always actually did — no behaviour change. **Self-inflicted miss caught before commit**: placing `_T = TypeVar("_T")` above the `from ..domain.entities.dataset import (...)` block tripped ruff `E402` (module level import not at top of file), and v3/v5 needed `ruff format`; both fixed before pushing, and re-verified on the real gates. mypy: the 10 `[list-item]` findings are gone and the edited file's ruff findings went 12 → 10 in the forks. Suites green: lab 920 unit + 79 integration, v3/v5 849 + 60, unchanged. **CI green on all four repos** (lab 7 jobs, v1 6, v3 6, v5 7, 0 non-success). v5's base advanced to `520389f` from a parallel session's release commit underneath my change; post-rebase suites re-verified. **Remaining defect-shaped counts are now 120/77/121/121 (lab/v1/v3/v5) and every one has been individually classified** — the rest is pure annotation debt (`type-arg`, `no-untyped-def`, `no-any-return`, `unused-ignore`, and the 53-91 `[call-arg]` that are the stdlib-`logging` vs structlog false positives), which carries no defect signal.


- Round 66 (authshield family: **v5's release pipeline had round-65's `dist/*` bug live, and securescan-pro-x's release was a permanent draft** `520389f` v5 / `4111003` securescan): applied round 65's lesson fleet-wide rather than assuming the other repos were clean. **`gh search code` returned empty for strings known to exist** (e.g. `softprops/action-gh-release`), so a code-search sweep would have been a **false negative** — the same trap as round 58's narrow grep, and round 60's lesson that absence of evidence is not evidence of absence. Swept deterministically via the contents API instead: enumerated all **28 org repos** × every `.github/workflows` file (`type=="file"` filter, per round-55's `gh api` 404 trap), filtered to the **12 release workflows**, and dumped **every** `files:` value. Coverage **proven** (`repos enumerated: 28 | with workflows: 28 | release workflow files: 12`) rather than assumed. **authshield-lab-v5 was the one live instance left**: byte-identical broken shape to the lab's pre-fix file — `sha256sum ./*` behind an `ls ./*` guard (so the guard meant to detect a *missing* build instead **failed the job on the `backend/`+`frontend/` directories** the artifact download creates), and `files: dist/*` against a `dist/` holding only directories, i.e. it would have attached **nothing at all**. Fixed with the same flat-packaging shape as the lab (tar the backend dist, zip the web dist excluding `linux-unpacked/`+installers, copy each installer out, `rm -rf dist/backend dist/frontend` so the glob *can only* match regular files, `find -maxdepth 1 -type f` checksums, `exit 1` when empty), collapsed the release job's two downloads into one `release-assets` artifact, validated the YAML, and **proved the layout locally before pushing**: packaging ran clean, `dirs left: 0`, the 3 packaged assets present, `sha256sum -c` 3/3 OK. No tag or release added — v5 has **never been released and has no tags**, so this makes the *first* release correct instead of shipping a broken one. `520389f` pushed, CI + Security Scan green on that sha (`36595508456`, `36595508365`). **securescan-pro-x: a different instance of the same family** — its release job passed `draft: true` and **no workflow in the repo ever un-drafted it** (proved by grepping every workflow for `draft=false`: zero hits), so its first `v*` tag would have produced an invisible, undownloadable release that never becomes `latest`. Switched to `draft: false` with a comment recording why; YAML parsed and the parsed release step asserted `draft=False` + its two already-correct recursive `dist/**/*` globs. Repo has no tags/releases, so nothing shipped under the old setting; deliberately did **not** invent a first version tag. `4111003` pushed. **Ruled out, not assumed:** securepacket (`artifacts/**/*` + `checksums.txt`) and secureport (`**/SecurePort-*.tar.gz`) already use recursive `**` globs that match only files; robin's `release.yml` is asset-less by design (commit-message notes, draft→publish) while `docker-release.yml` and betterleaks's release are container pushes with no `files:` key at all. No remaining `files:` glob in the org can drop a directory.

- Round 65 (authshield family: **both web-repo releases were structurally broken — fixed, re-tagged, re-released** `ca221fa` lab / `0f1e8e7` cipherforge): round 62's two v1.0.2 releases "succeeded" but **published nothing usable**, and the root cause was shared: `softprops/action-gh-release` with `files: dist/*` globs **files only**, so any *directory* under `dist/` is silently skipped — no warning, no non-zero exit. **(a) cipherforge-pro v1.0.2 was a dead shell**: it attached only `index.html`/`favicon.svg`/`icons.svg`, while the published `index.html` referenced `/assets/index-CVqom-1I.js` + `/assets/index-CnODaU5G.css`, **neither of which was in the release**. Proven rather than assumed, in both directions — downloaded the *released* `index.html` and read its `src`/`href` refs, then a local `vite build` showed exactly those two files in `dist/assets/` (the JS is **430 kB, i.e. the entire app**) while the release's asset list had no `assets/`. So the published v1.0.2 could only ever render a blank page. **(b) authshield-lab v1.0.2 attached only `SHA256SUMS.txt`** (same bug — `dist/` held the `backend/`+`frontend/` dirs the artifact upload creates), **and its manifest could never verify**: it listed nested paths like `./backend/dist/…` that were never published, so the release body's documented `sha256sum -c SHA256SUMS.txt` was guaranteed to fail for every user. **(c) A version lie rode along**: the tag said `v1.0.2` while every shipped binary self-reported `1.0.0-alpha.1` (frontend) / `1.0.0` (backend), and the release was marked **non-prerelease while shipping an alpha** — the "About lies" class again. Fixes: `frontend/package.json` + `backend/pyproject.toml` → `1.0.2`; authshield `release.yml` now **packages outputs into flat, versioned assets** (backend tarball; web zip excluding `linux-unpacked/` and the installers so nothing ships twice; each `*.AppImage/*.deb/*.rpm/*.snap` as its own file), checksums **exactly the attached files**, then `rm -rf dist/backend dist/frontend` so `files: dist/*` *can only* match regular files, and the release job consumes the new `release-assets` artifact; an empty manifest now `exit 1`s (fail-closed) instead of passing. cipherforge `release.yml` zips the whole `dist/` tree (making the body's "serve the `dist/` directory" literally true) + a `SHA256SUMS.txt` + a `## Verification` block. **Proven locally before pushing, in both repos** (reconstructing the exact nested CI artifact layout): packaging + checksum steps run clean, `find dist -mindepth 1 -type d` = **0** so the glob cannot skip anything, `sha256sum -c` passes, the extracted site contains **every** file the entrypoint references, and the `linux-unpacked`/AppImage/snap exclusions actually took. Gates re-run after the version bump: authshield 920 unit + 22 vitest + `tsc --noEmit` 0; cipherforge 100 vitest + `tsc -b` 0 + `vite build` clean. **Tag moved** (round-49 precedent, and safe here because neither release was ever consumable — authshield had only a checksum file, cipherforge was a dead shell — so no real artifact was overwritten): authshield `b209121 → 8cdf4ca → ca221fa`, cipherforge `8a33cf9 → 0f1e8e7`. **Results:** authshield run `36590974475` all 4 jobs success, release `v1.0.2` = backend tarball (1,149,793 B) + web zip (393,791 B) + `authshield-lab-1.0.2.AppImage` (149,242,935 B) + `authshield-lab_1.0.2_amd64.snap` (128,032,768 B) + `SHA256SUMS.txt`, with **all four API-reported sha256 digests matching the manifest exactly**; the two small archives were downloaded and verified (wheel+sdist now correctly named **1.0.2**; web zip contains `assets/`), and the AppImage/snap were magic-byte confirmed (x86-64 ELF, squashfs `hsqs`). cipherforge run `36592272367` success; release = `cipherforge-v1.0.2.zip` (120,443 B, `61d0673f…`) + `SHA256SUMS.txt`, zip verified to contain `dist/assets/index-CVqom-1I.js` + CSS + entrypoint; the 3 stale loose files the previous run left attached were **deleted** so the broken shell cannot be re-served. **Lesson:** a green release run plus a non-empty asset list proves neither that the pipeline is recursive nor that the release works — assert that every file the entrypoint references is *actually attached*, and verify the documented `sha256sum -c` against the **API-reported asset digests** (free, no 150 MB download).

- Round 64 (authshield family: **knowledge graph silently dropped prerequisite edges** — fixed in lab + v3 + v5 `90cb0f1` / `bd0275b` / `21498ef`): finished the v3/v5 `[arg-type]` triage, the class that exposed the shared EventBus crash in v1 (round 63). 25 findings per fork; 18 were the already-handled `_publish_event` shape, and 3 more were **benign** (verified, not assumed): `ScenarioDifficulty`/`ScenarioType` are `str, Enum` subclasses so a raw string compares equal to the member (`'beginner' == ScenarioDifficulty.BEGINNER` → True), and `tuple(sorted(<2 items>))` is always length 2, so the `set.add` finding is a mypy inference artifact, not a bug. That left the one genuinely broken site. **`get_node_graph()` de-duplicated edges with a single `seen_edges` set shared by two loops, keyed on `tuple(sorted((a, b)))` — a *direction-independent* pair.** That is correct for `related_nodes` (the sibling accessor marks those edges `bidirectional: True`), but prerequisites are **directed**: "B requires A" is not the same edge as "A relates to B". Because both loops wrote into the same key space, a prerequisite whose endpoints matched an already-seen related edge was **silently discarded** — no exception, no warning. Proven against the real service: node A with `related_nodes=["B"]` and node B with `prerequisites=["A"]` reported `edge_count=1` where 2 edges exist, so a renderer would draw correctly-shaped but semantically wrong output and `edge_count` under-reported the topology. The key now carries the edge kind, so the two spaces cannot collide while within-kind de-duplication is unchanged (related edges still collapse on the sorted pair; prerequisites dedupe on the exact directed pair). New `tests/unit/test_knowledge_graph_edges.py` (8) covers the collision, both dedup directions, opposite prerequisites, a true duplicate, the empty graph and `node_count`; **proven non-vacuous: 2 fail with the original direction-independent key restored**. One of my own test bugs caught and corrected: I first asserted that `A requires B` plus `B requires B` dedupe to one edge, but those are two genuinely different directed edges — the test now uses a node listing the same prerequisite twice. Unit 920/849/849 (+8 each), integration unchanged. Zero new lint debt (16 → 16 on the edited file, verified by stash + re-lint) and format-clean — this time `ruff format --check` was run **before** commit, deliberately closing last round's miss. **CI green on all four repos** (lab 7 jobs, v1/v3 6, v5 7, 0 non-success). The lab advanced to `ca221fa` from a parallel session's release work underneath this commit; my change and test are intact and the tree is clean. Fleet-wide latest-CI sweep (10 actively-maintained repos incl. the 3 Flutter apps, securescan-pro-x, cipherforge-pro, chevvy) is **all success**.

- Round 63 (authshield family: **EventBus.publish() crashed on any failing handler — fixed in all four repos** `e835d76` lab / `3b64b69` v1 / `9c7ba0e` v3 / `ba7ca2a` v5, + `ed07414`/`b209121`): finished the v1 triage, whose 19 `[arg-type]` findings were the one defect-shaped class round 59 had never examined (it had fixed only v1's profile-validator class). All 18 of those were the same shape — a `_publish_event(event: DomainEvent)` call site — and tracing it revealed a **shared-infrastructure crash in the event bus itself**. `EventBus.publish` is documented as isolating handler failures ("a single handler failure is logged but does **not** prevent other handlers from running"), but `DomainEvent.event_type` is typed as the `EventType` enum while **every** domain event subclass declares it as a plain `str` (`event_type: str = "audit.event_recorded"`). The except branch logged `event.event_type.value`, so handling a failing handler raised `AttributeError: 'str' object has no attribute 'value'` **from inside the except block** — one bad handler became an unhandled AttributeError that aborted `publish()` itself, abandoned every handler queued after it, *and* lost the original handler error. **Proven against the real bus in all four repos** before the fix (each `*** AttributeError: 'str' has no attribute 'value'`); after, the log line renders whichever form it was given (`getattr(_et, "value", _et)`), the handler error is logged normally, `publish()` returns, and later handlers still run. New `tests/unit/test_event_bus_handler_isolation.py` (5) covers both the str- and enum-`event_type` forms, asserts later handlers still run and the event still reaches the circular buffer; **proven non-vacuous: 3 fail with the original deref restored**. Unit 912/454/841/841 (+5 each) + integration 79/60/60/60, all green. **Inherited a live CI red on the lab and root-caused it**: the Lint job failed with an npm `ERESOLVE` that had nothing to do with the event-bus change — a wave of dependabot majors had landed and they are *mutually* incompatible: `@types/react-dom@19` needs peer `@types/react@^19` (project pinned ^18); `react-dom@19` needs peer `react@19` (project pinned ^18 — a broken hybrid that would fail at runtime too); `typescript@7.0.2` is outside `@typescript-eslint/eslint-plugin@8.70.1`'s declared `>=4.8.4 <6.1.0`; `vite@8.3.1` is outside `@vitejs/plugin-react@4.7.0`'s `^4.2 || ^5 || ^6 || ^7`. **`--legacy-peer-deps` does install, but only masks it** — with that flag eslint still fails to load its plugins at all and tsc 7 rejects the project's own tsconfig ("Option 'baseUrl' has been removed"), so the toolchain was genuinely broken. Rolled each back to the last coherent, previously-validated set (`react-dom`/`@types/react-dom` → ^18.2.0, `typescript` → ^5.9.3, `vite` → ^6.4.3, the exact version validated in round-55 follow-up #6); **no application source touched** (the renderer only uses `createRoot`, present in both React 18 and 19). Verified against the real toolchain, not just the manifest: `npm install` resolves with no ERESOLVE and no legacy flag, `tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint src` **0 errors**, `vite build` clean, `vitest --run` 22/22. **Self-inflicted miss caught and fixed**: I had verified `ruff check` on the new test but not `ruff format --check`, and the Lint job runs format strictly — one multi-line `pytest.param` turned the run red (`b209121`/`7701c06`/`981b91d`/`52f27b1` after `ruff format`). Also corrected an earlier false measurement: my "before=0 after=0" lint comparison used a grep that did not match ruff 0.16.9's output format; the corrected count shows 15 → 15 on `event_bus.py` in each fork, i.e. genuinely zero new debt. **CI green on all four**: lab 7 jobs, v1/v3 6 jobs each, v5 7 jobs, 0 non-success.

- Round 62 (login/account work shipped on **all three repos**; securepass **v2.2.30 AAB+APK released**): user asked to "clean the login to be able to create new users and play store executable". **(1) securepass-pro** — the login was email/password + consent only, with no way to create an account (the PIN `6ba7674` was replaced by email/password `a18cbfe`) and **no way to sign out at all**, so a user could never switch accounts. Shipped `5027685`: new `lib/features/login/domain/auth_service.dart` (PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256, 210k iterations, random 16-byte salt, constant-time compare, stored as `pbkdf2:<iter>:<saltB64>:<hashB64>`, in-place migration of legacy `base64` creds, `pbkdf2IterationsOverride` test seam, `hasAccount`/`signedInEmail`); new `/register` screen (email/password/confirm + mandatory consent, auto-authenticates on success); `login_screen` gained a full-width **Create Account** button; onboarding's **Skip Setup** became a *real* skip (guest, no credential) instead of fabricating an account; `app_router` now gates the **whole shell** on `AuthService().hasAccount` (public = `/onboarding`, `/login`, `/register`) instead of only `/home`; `settings_screen` converted `StatelessWidget → ConsumerStatefulWidget` with an Account tile (Sign Out + signed-in email / Sign In). **Found and fixed a real auth defect while testing**: the KDF hashed the **password alone**, so a wrong email with the right password verified `true` — now derives over `email:password`. **Fixed a pre-existing corruption in `login_screen.dart`'s build** (an earlier button insert had left an extra `],` splitting the `children` list, so the *Sign In* button was not actually inside the Column). New tests `test/auth_service_test.dart` (7) + `test/register_screen_test.dart` (4) — **73/73 pass, `flutter analyze` 0**. Two of my own test bugs fixed rather than papered over: buttons sat below the 600px test fold (needed `ensureVisible`), and `_acceptConsent` was a *toggle* so a second sign-in **un-ticked** consent (made idempotent) — the false failure it produced looked exactly like a consent-flow bug. Version trap avoided: **tag `v2.2.29` already existed** with pubspec still `2.2.28+33`, so the release is **`2.2.30+34`** (`AppConstants.appVersion '2.2.30'`). CI run `36532965292` success; artifact `11017488272` (96,007,552 B) → AAB `4e0ecdbf…1526` / APK `c5f5e327…50f0`, apksigner SHA-1 `7db5348e…` (anya-release-key-v2), `versionName 2.2.30` / `versionCode 39`, both download URLs 200, **`readiness-check.sh --releases` 6/6 pass 0 fail**. A parallel session raced the release: it `rm -f`'d my finished download and created the release first — its assets were **byte-identical** (digests match), but its notes **described an implementation that does not exist** (Riverpod `AsyncValue`, Google sign-in, a full-name field, a confirmation dialog, wrong test counts) → replaced the body with accurate notes via `gh release edit`. **(2) cipherforge-pro** `8a33cf9` — new `src/core/security/authManager.ts` (PBKDF2 150k + per-user salt + constant-time compare + legacy migration + persisted session + consent timestamps), rewritten `LoginScreen` (explicit signIn/create modes, confirm-password, consent blocks both, uniform "Invalid email or password."), session restore at mount, Header **Sign out**, PRIVACY_POLICY.md + README updated. **100 tests pass (24 new)**, `tsc -b` + vite build clean; quirk: `npm run typecheck` is a **no-op** (root tsconfig is a solution file with `files: []`) — `tsc -b` in the build is the real gate. **(3) authshield-lab** `2a1ce62` — real login/register wired with protected routes, session store, sign-out; `tsc --noEmit` exit 0, lint 0 errors, vitest 22/22, electron build OK. Tagged **`v1.0.2` on both web repos** (their `release.yml` triggers on `v*` and creates a GitHub Release; cipherforge's Firebase deploy step is `continue-on-error` so it can't block).

- Round 61 (authshield family: **all 4 of round-40's AttributeError crashes ported to v3 + v5** `552585c` / `8178632`): after round-60's false negative, stopped pattern-guessing and **let mypy enumerate the defect-shaped classes directly** on every fork (`mypy app --strict`, then filter to `[operator|union-attr|index|attr-defined|call-arg|arg-type|override|return-value|comparison-overlap|assignment|list-item|misc]`). v1 has 277 app/ errors, v3/v5 2318 each (identical defect sets). That immediately surfaced what rounds 58-60 had missed: **v3 and v5 still carried every one of the 4 live `AttributeError` crashes round-40 found in the lab** — `[attr-defined]` x6 on `app/optimization/services/release_governance_service.py:139,153` (`IReleaseRepository has no attribute update_approval`), `app/optimization/services/observability_extended_service.py:129` (`IDiagnosticTraceRepository has no attribute update`), `app/lms/services/competency_service.py:93` (`ICompetencyRepository has no attribute create_competency_progress`), and `app/quality/services/performance_service.py:68` (`BenchmarkRepository has no attribute find_all`). **Proven empirically** against the real in-memory repos before the fix: `CompetencyService.start_competency(...)` → `AttributeError: 'InMemoryCompetencyRepository' object has no attribute 'create_competency_progress'` (the `update_progress("", {}) or create_competency_progress(...)` paper-over is the round-40 finding verbatim — no record is ever keyed `""`, so the left side ALWAYS returned None and the right side never existed, meaning the *first* call always raised) and `PerformanceService.get_benchmarks(None)` → `AttributeError: 'InMemoryBenchmarkRepository' object has no attribute 'find_all'`. Fixed exactly as the lab did: `create_progress` / `find_all` / trace `update` / `update_approval` are now declared on their ABCs **and** implemented on the in-memory repos; the service calls `create_progress` directly and the always-None paper-over is gone. New `tests/unit/test_repository_interface_contracts.py` (8) drives the **real** in-memory repos on purpose (a `MagicMock` fabricates any attribute on demand, which is exactly why these survived a fully green suite — same reasoning as round-40) — **proven non-vacuous: 6 failures + 2 errors with the original shape restored**. Caught and fixed two of my own test bugs rather than papering over them: `rollback_publish` in the round-60 test needed a `published`-status fake, and the competency test asserted reuse-on-restart when the service deliberately raises `ValueError` for an already-`in_progress` learner. mypy: all 6 attr-defined resolved; the remaining ones are the known-benign `template_studio_service` str.get and 2 pre-existing operator + 3 override. Unit 836 (+8) + integration 60 in both forks. **Zero new lint debt** (44 → 44 findings across the 7 edited files, verified by stash + re-lint). **CI green: v3 run `36533185606` (6 jobs) and v5 run `36533189475` (7 jobs), 0 non-success.**

- Round 60 (authshield family: **publish-center None-deref crashes ported to v3 + v5** `11bcc6f` / `8c3bf33`): re-checked the sibling forks for the round-56/57 defect *classes* and caught that my round-58 grep was too narrow -- it only matched the single-line form, while the real defect was multi-line. Re-running with the correct shape found that **v3 and v5 still carried all 4 of the round-57 `publishing_center_service` None-dereference crashes** (v1 does not have those sites). Same defect: the repo's `update()` is typed `dict[str, Any] | None` and returns None when the row is missing, while each of `start_validation` / `sign_and_publish` / `reject_publish` / `rollback_publish` dereferenced `updated.get("updated_at", "")` *before* the `return updated or existing` fallback the same method already wrote. **Proven empirically** against a repository whose `update()` returns None: `start_validation` and `reject_publish` both raised `AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'get'` in v5 before the fix, and both return the pre-update record after. Applied the same 4-site fix used in the lab (`result = updated or existing`, resolved once and used for both the history entry and the return) via a method-aware transform that only touches the 4 crashing methods -- the other `or existing` sites that never dereferenced `updated` are untouched. **False-negative lesson recorded**: the round-58 conclusion ('pattern absent') was accidentally right for the *session* validators (the forks use the correctly-nested `if x is not None:` form, so there was no crash) but the narrow regex could not have detected a multi-line variant, so absence of evidence was not evidence of absence. New `tests/unit/test_publishing_center_none_deref.py` (4) drives the real service against a vanishing-row repository; **proven non-vacuous: all 4 fail with the original deref restored**. Suites green: v3 and v5 both 828 unit (+4) + 60 integration. **Zero new lint/format debt** -- verified by stashing and re-linting the edited file, findings byte-identical before and after (0 -> 0 lint, 2 -> 2 format). **CI green: v3 run `36531367227` (6 jobs) and v5 run `36531370005` (7 jobs incl. Integration Tests + Build), 0 failures each.**

Grepped all 11 Python dependency manifests in `/home/anya/coffee/fixes/*` for the httpx/starlette conflict class first — v1/v3/v5 pin `httpx==0.26.0` against the same `fastapi==0.109.2`, which is *compatible* (0.26 still accepts `app=`), so no fleet-wide CI break there. Integration Tests + Build). v5 needed a `git pull --rebase` first (a dependabot `@vitejs/plugin-react` merge landed mid-push); post-rebase suites re-verified.

- Round 55 follow-up #8 (darkdump **nltk alert dismissed with evidence — org hits 0 alerts fleet-wide**): with v1/v3 at 0 after follow-up #7, the last open alert was darkdump's nltk `GHSA-8mgp-746c-j5xp` ("Model-artifact APIs bypass pathsec", High 7.0, path-traversal/link-following CWE-22/59/73). Applied the follow-up #7 lesson (don't assume `patched=null` = floor) and verified BOTH ways: (1) **unfixable upstream** — range `<= 3.10.3` and the repo already pins `nltk==3.10.3`, which IS the newest release; `first_patched_version=null`, so no upgrade exists. (2) **unreachable in this repo** — darkdump.py only calls `stopwords.words`, `word_tokenize`, `FreqDist` (darkdump.py:426-437, all pure local NLP). It **never invokes the vulnerable surface** (`nltk.download`, `DataFinder`, `ModelFinder`, decompress/loader APIs); repo-wide grep for `download|DataFinder|ModelFinder` confirmed zero nltk hits. So this is a genuine not-used. **Dismissed via the proper API** (`gh api -X PATCH .../dependabot/alerts/31 -f state=dismissed -f dismissed_reason=not_used -f dismissed_comment=...`; gotcha: the comment field is capped at **280 chars**, first attempt 422'd on a 491-char comment) → `state=dismissed reason=not_used`. **FINAL FLEET STATE: 28 repos, 0 open Dependabot alerts, 0 open PRs, 0 open issues.** Re-verified by looping all 28 names and summing alert counts = 0. AGENTS "darkdump 1 (nltk genuine)" references should read 0. Note darkdump's pin could simply be pinned forward whenever nltk ships a patched release; the dismissal remains honest (unreachable surface) rather than a suppression.

- Round 55 follow-up #7 (v1/v3 **extract-zip alerts eliminated for real — electron 39.8.10 → 44.4.5**, `8c015da` / `3c4bf12`): follow-up #6's "extract-zip ×2 is the unfixable floor" conclusion was WRONG as an absolute — it was only unfixable *within* the pinned electron chain. Root cause found by diffing v1/v3's lockfile against lab's: **`electron` itself depends on `extract-zip ^2.0.1`** (v1/v3 had `39.8.10`; electron ≥40 dropped extract-zip from its own deps), so the alerts are bound to the pinned electron version, and lab already shipped electron **44.4.5** in this exact codebase (lab lockfile: extract-zip ABSENT, electron-builder 26.15.3 unchanged, `npm audit` 0) with CI green. User approved the bump — it was in their deferred major-version lane — via explicit question. **Verified with the real toolchain, not lockfile-regen alone**: each repo `npm install --package-lock-only` → extract-zip ABSENT + electron 44.4.5 + **`npm audit` 0 vulnerabilities (was 2 high)**; `npm install` (electron binary actually downloaded, reports `v44.4.5`); `npx tsc --noEmit` exit 0; `npx vite build` clean; **`npx electron-builder --linux dir` full packaging PASS** (linux-unpacked produced, exit 0 — the "duplicate dependency references" wall is electron-builder's standard info line, not an error). Commits `8c015da` (v1) + `3c4bf12` (v3), message `fix(deps): bump electron to 44.4.5 to drop extract-zip and close remaining advisories`. Committed ONLY the two frontend files — both repos had a **parallel session's uncommitted `backend/app/shared/events/event_bus.py` + `backend/tests/unit/test_event_bus_handler_isolation.py`** (the round-61 `EventBus.publish()` crash fix), left untouched; v3 needed `git stash -u` → rebase → push → `stash pop` to land mine on top of their round-61 commit `552585c` without colliding, parallel files restored byte-identical. **CI + Security Scan success on both electron commits** (`8c015da`, `3c4bf12`), and the parallel session's EventBus fix landed on top (`3b64b69` v1 / `9c7ba0e` v3, CI success) → **v1/v3 alerts 2 → 0**. **Final fleet state (all 28 repos): 0 open PRs, 0 open issues, only darkdump retains a single alert (nltk `patched=null`, genuinely unfixable) — every Dependabot advisory that has a fix is now closed org-wide.** Lesson sharpened from #6: `patched=null` on a transitive package is NOT proof of an unfixable floor — check whether a *parent* (here `electron`) carries it and whether a newer parent drops it; the "two-alert floor" was itself a reasoning defect.

- Round 55 follow-up #6 (v1/v3 **vite/esbuild alerts closed with a direct vite 6.4.3 bump** — the last fixable alert class in the fleet): with the merge wave done, v1 + v3 each sat at the **6-alert floor** (extract-zip ×2 + vite ×3 + esbuild ×1) and the "vite/esbuild stay in the user's PR #9 lane" constraint **no longer held — those PRs (v1 #9, v3 #5/#10) were ALL closed unmerged** (verified `state=closed merged=false`), so the alerts were open/fixable with no PR in flight, which satisfied round-55's "direct bumps only where no PR existed". Both repos' `frontend/package.json` had `vite: ^5.1.0`, lockfile esbuild **0.21.5**; the shared frontend repair (`a4aa003`/`aa07d4a`) had ALREADY installed `@vitejs/plugin-react@4.7.0`, so the delta was a one-line manifest edit. **Commits `11818b2` (v1) + `54fcac1` (v3)**, identical `fix(deps): bump vite to 6.4.3 to close vite and esbuild advisories`, done on the same shared baseline lab used (`^5.1.0`→`^6.4.3`, exactly like `1b6d685`/`c5301dc`). **Verified locally with the real toolchain before committing** (not just lockfile-regen): each repo got `npm install --package-lock-only` → lockfile resolved **vite 6.4.3 + esbuild 0.25.12** (matches lab), remaining `npm audit` = exactly the 2 unfixable extract-zip (`patched=null`), `npx tsc --noEmit` exit 0, `npx vite build` clean (65 modules, dist/ built, then `rm -rf`'d). Pushed `4804fb4..11818b2` and `f106172..54fcac1`. **CI (run 32 both) all jobs success** — v1 (6/6: Test×2, Type Check×2, Lint×2), v3 (6/6); **Security Scan success on both heads** (`11818b2`, `54fcac1`). **Alerts dropped 6→2 on each: only extract-zip ×2 remain (upstream `patched=null`, the genuinely unfixable floor).** Final fleet state: authshield-lab 0 PRs/0 alerts, v1 0/2, v3 0/2, v5 0/0, securescan-pro-x 0/0, hacker-roadmap 0/0 — **every fixable alert across all 28 repos is now closed**; remaining = v1/v3 extract-zip ×2 (unfixable) + darkdump nltk ×1 (`patched=null`). Also learned: Dependabot auto-closes its security PRs when the manifest/lockfile drifts past them, so "parked-in-PR-lane" reasoning can silently rot — re-query `pulls?state=closed` before trusting a lane.

- Round 58 (authshield-lab: **unimportable `app.defenses.policy` package + httpx/starlette pin conflict fixed**, `cd84013`→`77c946f` and `5455131`): **(1) `app/defenses/policy/` was entirely dead code** — three modules used 3-dot relative imports to reach `app.shared`, but at their depth 3 dots resolve to `app.defenses`, which does not exist: `policy_engine.py` (`from ...shared.events.event_bus`, `...shared.monitoring.performance`) and `validators/policy_validator.py` (`...shared.validation.validator`). Both targets exist at `app/shared/…`, so each needed a 4th dot. Since the package's own `__init__.py:47` imports the engine, `import app.defenses.policy` raised `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'app.defenses.shared'`. It was NOT a live startup crash only because nothing outside `app/defenses/` imports it — the suite stayed green the whole time — and it was the source of 13 of the 3391 mypy `import-not-found` errors, which is part of what buried the real signal. Now imports cleanly (38 exports) and makes the round-56 `policy_validator` fixes reachable/testable for the first time. **(2) The rebase onto origin/main pulled in a dependabot ruff bump (0.15.22→0.16.9) AND exposed a live CI red**: `Integration Tests` failed with 19 errors `TypeError: Client.__init__() got an unexpected keyword argument 'app'`. Root cause is a **mutually unsatisfiable pin pair**: `requirements.txt` had `httpx==0.28.1`, but `fastapi==0.109.2` requires `starlette>=0.36.3,<0.37.0`, and starlette 0.36.x's `TestClient` passes `app=` straight through to `httpx.Client` — which **httpx 0.28.0 removed** (deprecated test-only shortcut). Proved by unpacking all three wheels and reading `Client.__init__` directly: 0.26.0 `app=` **True**, 0.27.2 **True**, 0.28.1 **False**. Pinned `httpx==0.27.2` (last release accepting it) with an explanatory comment. **Root cause of why it was invisible locally: the venv had drifted to httpx 0.26.0, which still accepts `app=`, so the local suite was green and structurally could not surface it — any local httpx <0.28 will keep hiding this, which makes the pin the thing that keeps local and CI agreeing.** Reproduced both directions on the same suite: 0.28.1 → 60 passed/19 errors; 0.27.2 → 79 passed. Also completed the defect-shaped mypy triage: the remaining production `attr-defined` (`template_studio_service.py:177` `.get` on a str) is **runtime-correct** (verified by driving the real repo+service) and the 92 `call-arg` are **false positives** — the code uses stdlib `logging`, whose `.info()` accepts arbitrary `**kwargs`; mypy resolves to structlog's stricter `Logger`. The remaining 17 `union-attr`/`index` are all in `tests/` (assertion warts). **No further live crash classes remain in app/.** ruff clean under the newly pinned 0.16.9 (662 files); unit 907 + integration 79 (unit rose 880→907 from a *parallel* session's commit `37ece01`, not from these changes — verified by re-running collect-only with the fix stashed and restored, which yields an identical count). CI all 7 jobs success + Security Scan success on `5455131`. **Landed alongside a parallel session** editing the same tree: staged/committed only my own files, stashed and restored their uncommitted `frontend/` work untouched.

- Round 57 (authshield-lab: **profile-validator TypeError crash + is_valid bypass + 4 None-derefs fixed** `615cb97`): second defect-shaped harvest, this time the `[union-attr]` and `[call-arg]` classes. **(a) `validate_profile_update` crashed on ANY payload containing a `display_name`** — it called `Validator.validate_length(..., min_length=1, max_length=64)` but that method's parameters are named `min_len`/`max_len`, so every call raised `TypeError: validate_length() got an unexpected keyword argument 'min_length'`. The profile validator crashed on the most basic valid input (`{'display_name': 'Ada'}`). **(b) A silent validation BYPASS in the same call site**: it accumulated failures with `result.errors.extend(...)` instead of `result.merge(...)`. `ValidationResult.is_valid` is a plain bool that only flips inside `add_error()`/`merge()`, so extending the list directly left `is_valid == True` *with* a non-empty errors list — `validate_profile_update({'display_name': ''})` returned `is_valid=True, errors=1`. Any caller gating on `.is_valid` accepted an invalid profile. Now uses `merge()`. **(c) Four None-dereferences in `publishing_center_service.py`** (`start_validation`/`sign_and_publish`/`reject_publish`/`rollback_publish`): the repo's `update()` is typed `dict[str, Any] | None` and returns `None` when the row is missing, and each method called `updated.get("updated_at", "")` *before* the `return updated or existing` fallback those same methods already wrote — the author demonstrably expected None, but the deref came first → `AttributeError`. Each now resolves `result = updated or existing` once and uses it for both the history entry and the return; the 4 other `or existing` sites that never dereferenced `updated` are untouched. New `tests/unit/test_profile_update_validator.py` (7 tests: wrong-kwarg TypeError regression + an is_valid/errors consistency sweep over 8 payloads) — **proven non-vacuous: 3 fail with the original code restored**. mypy: the 4 `publishing_center_service` union-attrs are gone; the `call-arg` class drops 94 → 92. ruff clean (658 files). **Unit 880 passed (was 873), integration 60 passed** — no regressions. Landed on top of a rebase over 3 dependabot merges (`3677423` pytest-cov 7.1.0, `20084cb` tailwind-merge, `b8e83a7` react-dom) — post-rebase suites re-verified green. CI + Security Scan both success, all 7 CI jobs green.

- Round 56 (authshield-lab: **7 validator None-comparison crashes fixed + shipped** `f910115`): continued round-40's tactic of triaging only the *defect-shaped* mypy slice of the 3391 `--strict` errors rather than grinding the annotation mass. Grepping the `[operator]` class found one anti-pattern repeated 7 times: `if (value is not None and not isinstance(value, int)) or value < 1:`. Python's `or` evaluates each clause independently, so the `is not None` guard protected only the FIRST clause — and `dict.get()` returns `None` for an absent key, so any caller that simply omitted the field executed `None < 1` → `TypeError: '<' not supported between instances of 'NoneType' and 'int'`. **The validation layer crashed on the most common input (a payload that doesn't mention an optional field) instead of validating it.** Reproduced against the REAL functions: `validate_session_data({})` → TypeError; `validate_session_data({'user_id':'u1','idle_timeout_minutes':30})` → TypeError (crash just *moves* to the next field, proving the guard never worked); `validate_session_filters({'per_page': None})` → TypeError; `validate_assessment_data({})` → TypeError. Sites: `session_validator.py` idle_timeout_minutes/security_level/per_page, `lms_validator.py` time_limit_minutes, `policy_validator.py` priority ×2 + risk_weight. Fix = move the range comparisons INSIDE the `is not None` guard (absent → skipped, present-but-invalid → still rejected). **Also closed a validation bypass**: page/per_page used `if page is not None and (...)`; since `.get` already supplies the default (1/20) the guard never helped, so an explicit `page: None` skipped validation entirely and was handed to the repository unchecked — now an explicit error, and both reject `bool` (Python treats `True` as an in-range int). New `tests/unit/test_validator_optional_numerics.py` (32 tests, real validators, no mocks) — **proven non-vacuous: 9 fail with the original guard shape restored**. mypy `--strict` 3391 → 3365; the `operator` class drops 18 → 3 (remaining 3 are pre-existing `scalar() > 0` in `role_repository` that already carry explicit ignores). ruff check + format clean (657 files). **Unit 873 passed, integration 60 passed** — no regressions. CI run `36460985784` all 7 jobs success (Lint, Type Check, Unit Tests, Integration Tests, Security Scan, Accessibility Checks, Build); Security Scan `36460985715` success. Side finding (not fixed, dead code): `app/defenses/policy/` is **unimportable** — its `services/policy_engine.py` does `from ...shared.events.event_bus import ...` which resolves to the nonexistent `app.defenses.shared`; the package's own `__init__.py` imports it. Nothing outside `app/defenses/` imports that package, so it is dead rather than a live startup crash, and it also explains 13 of the `import-not-found` mypy errors.

(1) **Rules fix** (`3ffb9e4`): `match /users/{userId}` contained `match /{document=**}` (recursive wildcard). A recursive wildcard matches ZERO segments too → it also matched the parent doc `users/{uid}`, granting the owner unconditional read/**write** on their own user document, bypassing `isValidUserData`/`isValidUserUpdate`/`hasNoPrivilegeFields` — users could write `role`/`permissions`/fake `email`/`createdAt` to their own doc (empirically reproduced via emulator: `set hacked-only` and `set role:'admin'` on `users/user-A` both SUCCEEDED; cross-user `users/user-B` still DENIED). App only uses top-level `users`+`scan_history`, so the wildcard was pure risk → removed (deny-all fallback covers future subcollections). (2) **Added scan's missing rules-testing suite**: `firebase/rules-testing/` mirrors radhika's (devDeps `@firebase/rules-unit-testing ^5.0.2`, `firebase-admin ^12.7.0`, `firebase-tools 15.29.0`, project `scan-translate-ai`), **17 tests**: F1 anon-denied, F2 cross-user denied, F3 owner app-shapes allowed (incl. the `role`-field **rejection** that now passes post-fix), F4 role-escalation denied, F5 forged userId denied, F6 invalid scanType/rawValue denied, F7 list-must-filter-userId, F8 cross-user update/delete denied + owner delete allowed, F9 admin-claim positive control via `{role:'admin'}` token, F10 deny-all fallback (now also asserts `users/{uid}/subs` denied), F10b cross-user user-doc delete denied, S1–S5 Storage (anon denied, cross-user isolation, owner image writes ≤5MiB/10MiB image/*, admin-write public/world-read w/ seeded object, deny-all). Converted test-10's owner-allowed users-subcollection case to the new subcollection-denied assertion. `JAVA_HOME=/home/anya/tools/jdk21` (system java 25 breaks emulator). First run 13/17 → fixed 3 harness bugs (invalid 1-seg/3-seg doc refs `db.doc('scan_history')`, unmatched/deep/path; S4 needed a rules-disabled `putString` seed so read-checks see an existing object) → **17/17 green**. (3) **CI gates** added to BOTH workflows (`firebase-deploy.yml` = new `security-rules-tests` job + `deploy-rules: needs:` it; `play-store-release.yml` = same job mirroring radhika: zulu 21, node 20, npm ci, working-directory firebase/rules-testing). ## Objective
- Keep Radhika, Scan Translate AI, and SecurePass Pro safe, correct, and shipable as APK/AAB GitHub releases. Play Store upload is explicitly out of scope.
- Ongoing cycle: user pastes a commit message = "this commit contains the error" → audit that change for real defects → fix → verify → bump version → release.

Sizes: radhika ~99MiB, securepass ~88MiB, scantranslate ~200MiB. **Gotchas learned:** output file's parent dir must already exist; don't pass `Authorization` header to Azure blob (403); big blobs can hang the range curl forever → `dl.sh` now uses `--connect-timeout 30 --max-time 400` per attempt so hung connections abort and resume; run downloads sequentially (parallel runs produced 0 bytes repeatedly).

- Round 45 (securepass external security review → **v2.2.27**, the backup/restore flow was fake): user pasted an Isaac Palaparthi-style SecurePass Pro security review (docs-vs-code, AdMob, crypto audit, clipboard, backup robustness, data-loss UX, version consistency). Audited every claim against the repo and found the crypto layer was **genuinely good** (AES-256-GCM via `AesGcm.with256bits()`, package-generated per-encryption nonce so no IV reuse, GCM tag verified on decrypt, PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 210k with `Random.secure()` salt persisted, legacy sha256→pbkdf2 vault-PIN migration covered by round 41's test, no plaintext logging) — but the **backup/restore path was almost entirely cosmetic**. Real defects fixed in commit `7d60ce6` (analyze 0, tests **50/50** up from 19): (1) **backups never contained the vault** — `createBackup()` counted entries in its size estimate but `exportBackup()` never wrote them, so "Create encrypted backup" produced an empty shell with no credentials in it; (2) **the app's own envelopes were unreadable** — `exportBackup()` wraps the versioned envelope one level deeper (`{metadata…, data:'{"v":1,"enc":true,"data":"<b64>"}'}`) than `_extractEnvelopeData()` understood, so a backup this app produced could not be restored by this app; (3) `validateImportData()` rejected the app's own keys (`config`/`workspaces`/`folders`/`vault`) so even a decrypted app-shaped payload reported invalid; (4) **restore was fire-and-forget** — the vault write ran under `unawaited()`, so "Restored N items" could be reported before anything persisted; now awaited, with a persistence failure reported as a *failure* instead of counted as a success; (5) envelope `v` was ignored (a future format would be decrypted on a guess) → explicit `supportedEnvelopeVersion` rejection; (6) `isEncrypted` defaulted to **false** so the default path wrote plaintext; (7) `validateBackup()`/`previewBackup()` cast `metadata`/`data` to `Map<String,dynamic>` unguarded → TypeError on a malformed backup instead of a clean report; (8) `getCompatibilityStatus()` string-compared versions against a hardcoded `'1.0.0'` (so "2.0" < "10.0" and every real build reported `may_require_update`) → `RuntimeInfoService` + numeric segment compare; (9) **clipboard auto-clear deadline was memory-only** → a secret stayed on the clipboard forever if the process was killed; the deadline is now persisted and an overdue one is cleared on the next launch. New tests: `test/backup_crypto_test.dart` (22: AES round-trip, unique nonce, ciphertext/tag tamper, truncation, bad Base64, empty, key persistence, valid/corrupt/foreign/unsupported-v99/large/duplicate envelopes, malformed metadata, app-produced shape) + `test/backup_roundtrip_test.dart` (2: **real `BackupService.exportBackup` → `ImportService` round-trip asserting vault entries are readable again afterwards**, so the awaited restore is *proven*, plus a decrypted-payload key check) + `test/clipboard_lifetime_test.dart` (7: overdue deadline cleared, future deadline not cut short, unparseable discarded, no-deadline no-op, copy persists a deadline, repeat copies refresh it, manual clear removes it). Two of my own test bugs were found and fixed rather than papered over: the `EnhancedClipboardService` `_initialized` guard made `initialize()` a no-op on the 2nd test in a process (exposed `clearStaleClipboardOnStartup` as `@visibleForTesting`), and I had asserted `vault`/`folders` are envelope keys when they are inside the ciphertext — the envelope is only `{v, enc, data}`. **Docs corrected where they were false**: PRIVACY claimed a "master password" (there is none — a device-generated key in secure storage) and "if you enable cloud backup, encrypted vault files are stored with the provider you select" (**there is no cloud provider and the app uploads nothing** — the envelope is copied to the clipboard; any third-party store is the user's own choice and applies its own policies); both READMEs said "no tracking" while AdMob's own docs state it processes the Advertising ID for ad serving/measurement (restated: no app server ever sees vault/password contents, AdMob is the one exception and never receives them). **Backups are device-bound** — the key is deliberately not in the file, so a backup restores only on the device that made it; that is now stated in README Known Limitations, PRIVACY §3, and in the Backup & Restore dialog itself (which previously implied cross-device portability and said nothing about uninstall). Bumped **2.2.27+32** (`b19906e`, also fixed the stale `AppConstants.appVersion` fallback `'1.0.0'` → `'2.2.27'`), tag v2.2.27, pushed; CI run 36232252946.

Full `mypy backend --strict` = **4227** errors (round 38's "2290" figure came from a narrower path), broken down as no-untyped-def 1144, type-arg 1033, no-any-return 461, no-untyped-call 176, unused-ignore 133, call-arg 114, import-not-found 80, plus ~44 defect-shaped (`attr-defined`/`union-attr`/`operator`/`override`/`return-value`/`index`/`comparison-overlap`). **The 44 were worth it: 4 were genuine runtime crashes** — services/routes calling concrete repo methods that existed on **neither the ABC nor the in-memory impl**: (1) `CompetencyService.start_competency` → `create_competency_progress` (papered over with `update_progress("", {}) or create_competency_progress(...)`, but no record is ever keyed `""`, so the first call always returned None and the `or` always raised; reachable via `lms_routes.py:674` + `InstructorService`, **zero test coverage**), (2) `PerformanceService.get_benchmarks(None)` → `BenchmarkRepository.find_all`, (3) `GET /a11y/scorecards` → `A11yScorecardRepository.find_all` (public HTTP GET), (4) `ObservabilityExtendedService.add_span_to_trace` → `IDiagnosticTraceRepository.update`. Fix: added `create_progress` / `find_all` / `find_all` / `update` to the respective ABCs **and** in-memory impls (key-allowlist updates matching the sibling `update_approval` convention; `create_progress` mints a uuid + timestamps like `create_competency`), and the service now calls `create_progress` directly. **2 more were interface-declaration bugs** where the impl was already correctly typed but the ABC said `object`: `IReleaseRepository` gained `update_approval`; `APIStabilityRepository` now declares `APIStabilityReport` (clearing 2 return-value + 1 override error and the now-obsolete `# type: ignore[return-value]` in `sustainability_service.py`; needed `APIStabilityReport` added to the interfaces `__init__` import). The last 2 (`template_studio_service.py:177`, `password_hasher.py:161`) are benign defensive-coding/typing warts, runtime-correct — left for the bulk cleanup. **Root cause of the blind spot (worth remembering):** the existing unit tests inject `MagicMock()` repos, and a MagicMock fabricates *any* attribute on demand — a method absent in production still "works" in tests, so all 877 tests stayed green through 4 live crashes. New `backend/tests/unit/test_repository_interface_contracts.py` (**24 tests**) uses the **real** in-memory repos so a missing method is a hard failure; **verified it actually catches the bugs** by stashing the source fixes and re-running: **14 failed** with exactly the predicted `AttributeError`s (the 10 that passed pre-fix are the interface-declaration-only ones, correctly runtime-safe), then restored. - Round 30 (scan cross-screen error isolation, v1.2.21 shipped, released — on-device proven): the round-28/29 "dispose clears shared error" fix does **not** work — an emulator walk on the shipped v1.2.20 build showed the camera-denied "Retry" banner STILL leaking onto the fresh Voice page, and logcat recorded `Bad state: Cannot use "ref" after the widget was disposed.` Root cause (Riverpod 2.6.1 semantics, source-verified at `consumer.dart:548`): by the time `State.dispose()` runs, the element's lifecycle is already `defunct` → **`ref.read()` in dispose throws, and mutating a provider from `initState`/`build`/`dispose` is forbidden** (debug assertion), so those changes silently never apply to the UI. The committed fix `f147faa` (`fix: never surface a previous screen's error and cancel mic via a cached datasource`, tag v1.2.21, **authored by the parallel session incl. my in-progress edits**) is the correct design and was verified on-device on the actual release: (1) camera-capture failures are written **only** to `ocrProvider` (page-local); `translationProvider.clearError()` on capture failure + combined banner `ocrState.errorMessage ?? transState.errorMessage` on the camera page → the shared translation state is never dirtied by a camera-denied flow; (2) voice/text/camera drop stale translation/OCR errors **post-frame** (`WidgetsBinding.instance.addPostFrameCallback` + `mounted` guard, NOT initState/dispose) so a fresh page never renders another screen's error; (3) voice page snapshots `translationRemoteDataSource` at `initState` (`late final _speechDatasource`) so dispose can cancel the mic session without touching ref (fixes the round-28 mic-cancel, which was firing a post-dispose exception on every real Back); (4) `test/banner_leak_test.dart` (2 tests) → suite 5/5 (was 1/1 + earlier translation_state tests). Verified `flutter analyze` 0 issues. On-device walk on **the actual v1.2.21 release build** (uninstall+reinstall after discovering the device silently kept running versionCode 30 of v1.2.20 — `install -r -d` did NOT downgrade; `adb uninstall` + clean `install` required; **always `dumpsys package ... versionCode` before trusting device QA**): sign-in `opencode.qa@scantranslateai.app`/`Radhika2026`, Home → Camera Translate → Camera → OS dialog → Don't allow → camera page shows its own "Try Again" (_Failed to capture image: PlatformException(camera_access_denied)_) → Back → Home → Voice Translate → **NO banner** (pre-fix build showed "Retry" here). 0 FATALs, no ref/dispose exceptions in the new process. v1.2.21 CI run 35751586379 SUCCESS; artifact 10707560561 (210,520,677 B) via dl.sh one pass; APK sha256 `e5c8e5a5…` / AAB `419cb440…` **byte-identical to the externally-created release (id 394382167) + its published Checksums block**; apksigner Signer SHA-1 `7db5348e…` = anya-release-key-v2 release-signed. v1.2.19 (release 393616067, APK `1634fbd8…`/AAB `e6789541…`) and v1.2.20 (release 393876825, AAB `731e89ce…`/APK `338393d2…`) also verified present with matching digests. Tracker totals unchanged (radhika 50/74, scan 45/74, securepass 53/74) — but the scan tracker's last open gap (stale camera-denied banner) is now closed via v1.2.21. **Post-ship mic-arm A/B on the same emulator (round-31 follow-up)**: the actual v1.2.21 release APK (versionCode 31) did NOT show "Listening…" after tapping the mic — logcat showed `Recognizer choice` + `gsaVis null binding` + GSA `errorCode: 401` + transient DNS "too many resolution errors". Downgraded to the v1.2.20 release APK (`install -r -d`, same release signature, versionCode 30) → **identical non-arm** on the same emulator state. Verdict: NOT a v1.2.21 regression — the emulator's Google speech backend is broken right now (network recognizer 401, no GSA binding; round-29 arming happened when the env was healthy). Arming code + `VoiceInputWidget` are byte-identical across v1.2.20→v1.2.21 (diff-verified), the app correctly initiates recognition on tap (logcat), and the emulator was left on the v1.2.21 release build.

Audited every area against code: consent service already the final round-13 design (cooldown dual-clock, fail-closed, no blocked-cache) — no change needed; storage caps + cleartext-off + on-device OCR/lookup already in place. **Real defects found + fixed** (commit `cee379f`, analyze 0, tests 3/3): (1) **privacy gate** — Acceptance button fired `setMustAccept`+navigate without persisting; an offline first-launch write silently navigated home while the backend still expected acceptance → next launch re-forced the gate (loop). `acceptPrivacyPolicy` now returns `Future<bool>`, gate awaits the Firestore write, only navigates on success, shows a retryable error + blocks double-taps otherwise; (2) **camera permission path** (`barcode_scanner_page`/`qr_scanner_page`) — permanently-denied permission dead-ended on a looping "Grant Permission" button (settings intent added via `_offerOpenSettings`), scanner had no `errorBuilder` (plugin defaults to a blank black box on camera-occupied/unavailable/unsupported) → new shared `ScannerErrorView` widget with message + Retry, and a `WidgetsBindingObserver` re-checks permission on resume (revoke-while-backgrounded no longer leaves a stale granted flag); (3) **voice** — backing out while listening left the mic recording up to the full 15s listenFor window (`dispose` now cancels the session), and permanently-denied mic routes to settings; (4) **shared-state leaks** — OCR/translation/voice wrote errors/results into the shared `ocrProvider`/`translationProvider` after their owning page was disposed (the round-25 "camera-denied banner persists across screens" defect): mounted guards added (text/ocr/camera-translate/voice) + camera-translate Camera/Gallery buttons disabled while a translation is in flight. Rewrote `ad_consent_service.dart` in **all three** repos: fail-closed (errors → ads off, uncached so flow retries next call), `required`/`unknown` after form → blocked, definitive `obtained`/`notRequired` → `canRequestAds()`. Same content at every repo (only doc comments differed). Analyze 0 errors + tests green on all three. Bumped **radhika 1.2.8+13, securepass 2.2.6+11, scantranslate 1.2.6+11**; commits `fix: UMP consent fails closed on errors instead of silently serving ads`; pushed tags v1.2.8 / v2.2.6 / v1.2.6; CI green; downloaded artifacts and created releases with APK+AAB — all verified.
- Error round 5 (consent): audited my own round-4 fix commit and found real defects in `ensureConsent()`: **(1)** any `false` from the flow (required + form unavailable, required + form-show failure, unknown) set `_finished = true` → blocked result cached for the whole session, flow never re-ran — an EEA first-launch user could be blocked all session with the form never shown, contradicting the commit's "retries until decisive / subsequent obtain grants ads" claim; **(2)** permanent hang if `requestConsentInfoUpdate`'s callbacks never fire (`_inFlight` stuck). Rewrote the service again (identical across repos, `cmp`-verified): cache **only** a definitive allow; never cache `false` so next call re-runs UMP; bounded in-call retry (2 attempts, 4s delay); 20s `_plumbingTimeout` on `requestConsentInfoUpdate` + form load only — **not** on interactive `form.show()`. Analyze 0 errors + tests green everywhere. Bumped **radhika 1.2.9+14, securepass 2.2.7+12, scantranslate 1.2.7+12**; commits `fix: don't cache blocked consent results so flow re-runs until decisive`; tags v1.2.9 / v2.2.7 / v1.2.7; CI green; downloaded artifacts (sequential; scantranslate needed dl.sh timeouts to avoid hang) and verified releases with AAB+APK on all three.
- Error round 6 (consent): audited my own round-5 fix commit and found the 4s in-call retry re-ran UMP on ANY blocked outcome — including "form shown but status still required/unknown" — so a user who just declined or dismissed the consent form was shown it again four seconds later (defect: retry must only cover transient pre-form failures). Rewrote `_run()` to return an explicit outcome record `({bool allowed, bool formShown})`: definitive allow (cached), form-shown block (final for this call, never re-presented), transient block (form unavailable / status unknown / thrown error → retried once after 4s, not cached). Identical across repos (`cmp`-verified). Analyze 0 errors + tests green everywhere. Bumped **radhika 1.2.10+15, securepass 2.2.8+13, scantranslate 1.2.8+13**; commit `fix: do not re-present the consent form after the user already decided`; tags v1.2.10 / v2.2.8 / v1.2.8; CI green; downloaded artifacts (sequential) and verified releases with AAB+APK on all three.
- Round 7 (v1.2.11 / v2.2.9 / v1.2.9): auditing round-6 commit found `show()` failure (FormError) was cached as a form-shown block → retry loop never re-offered → session-fatal. Fix: report failed presentation as transient (`formShown:false`), only a successful `show()` (error==null) marks the form as shown. Superseded before release by round 8.
- Round 8 (v1.2.12 / v2.2.10 / v1.2.10): caching ANY form-shown outcome as session-final meant `required`/`unknown` AFTER the form gave no recorded decision yet the block was cached for the whole session (round-5 bug resurrected). Fix: cache ONLY `allowed==true`; keep a `_formShownThisSession` latch so the flow re-runs next call while the form is never auto-presented twice. Superseded by round 9.
- Round 9 (v1.2.13 / v2.2.11 / v1.2.11): the session-lifetime `_formShownThisSession` latch was set even for a dismissal (non-decision) → flow re-runs forever but the only path to a decision is disabled (round-8's block "in a new costume"). Fix: replace the one-shot latch with a presentation rate limit — 10-min `_minDelayBetweenPresentations` cooldown via `_lastFormShownAt`. Superseded by round 10.
- Round 10 (v1.2.14 / v2.2.12 / v1.2.12): the DateTime `now().difference()` cooldown is wall-clock vulnerable — forward jump instantly lifts the limiter (re-prompt a just-dismissed user), backward jump extends forever (latch). Fix: monotonic `Stopwatch` (`_clock`, `_lastFormShownElapsed`). Superseded by round 11.
- Round 11 (v1.2.15 / v2.2.13 / v1.2.13): Stopwatch freezes during suspend — "dismiss, then background 10+ real min" never elapses the cooldown → silent re-latch (round-9 bug via clock source). Fix: dual clocks — measure `max(wall, monotonic)`. Superseded by round 12.
- Round 12 (v1.2.16 / v2.2.14 / v1.2.14): `max(wall, monotonic)` reintroduced round-10's forward-jump re-prompt: any forward wall jump (NTP/DST/clock-fix) inflates the wall term → instant re-prompt of a user who just dismissed the form. Fix (round 13): monotonic base + **observed-suspension credit** only — `with WidgetsBindingObserver`, credit `wallGap - monoGap` across real pause/resume cycles (`_suspendedCredit` reset on each presentation); no foreground clock jump can shorten the cooldown. Imports `flutter/widgets.dart` (for `WidgetsBindingObserver`) **+** `flutter/foundation.dart` (`kIsWeb` is NOT re-exported by widgets). Final design; verified.
